# List 16

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mingle</b></td><td>/ˈmɪŋɡl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>混合；与人交往</td><td>mingle with the crowd<br>混入人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Smoke mingled with fog above the crowded harbor.  烟雾与雾气在拥挤的港口上方交织。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mix</td><td>/ˈmɪks/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>混合，掺和</td><td>mix the ingredients<br>把配料混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mix the flour and water until the dough becomes smooth.  把面粉和水混合，直到面团变得光滑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amalgamate</td><td>/əˈmælɡəmeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>合并，混合</td><td>amalgamate two companies<br>合并两家公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two small colleges amalgamated to cut administrative costs.  两所小学院合并以削减行政成本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blend</td><td>/ˈblend/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>混合，融合</td><td>blend the colors smoothly<br>把颜色柔和地调和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two rivers blend into one wide brown stream.  两条河汇成一条宽阔的浑浊水流。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commingle</td><td>/kəˈmɪŋɡl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>充分混合</td><td>commingle funds<br>把资金混在一起</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer never commingles client funds with his own money.  这位律师从不把客户资金与自己的钱混在一起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 immingle</del></td><td>/ɪˈmɪŋɡəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>混合，融入</td><td>immingle with the crowd<br>混入人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voices from the street immingled with the music inside the hall.  街上的声音与厅内的音乐混在了一起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intermix</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈmɪks/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>掺杂，混合在一起</td><td>intermix with water<br>与水混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two populations intermixed freely over several centuries.  这两个族群在几个世纪里自由混居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 merge</td><td>/ˈmɜːrdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>合并，融合</td><td>merge two companies<br>合并两家公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two banks merged last year to cut operating costs.  这两家银行去年合并以削减运营成本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 divide</td><td>/dɪˈvaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>分开，划分；分歧</td><td>divide into groups<br>分成小组</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher divided the class into four groups of five.  老师把班级分成四个五人小组。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 separate</td><td>/ˈsepəreɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>分开，分离</td><td>separate the yolks<br>把蛋黄分离出来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please separate the recyclable waste from the ordinary rubbish.  请把可回收垃圾与普通垃圾分开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sunder</td><td>/ˈsʌndər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>分开，割断</td><td>sunder the alliance<br>使联盟破裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bitter dispute sundered an alliance that had lasted decades.  这场激烈的争端割断了维持数十年的联盟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>minimize</b></td><td>/ˈmɪnɪmaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刻意低估</td><td>minimize the risks<br>刻意淡化风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company tried to minimize the risks in its statement to worried investors.  公司在给焦虑投资者的声明中试图刻意低估风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deprecate</td><td>/ˈdeprəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不赞成，反对；贬低</td><td>deprecate his efforts<br>贬低他的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tends to deprecate his own achievements whenever anyone praises them.  每当有人称赞，他总倾向于贬低自己的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diminish</td><td>/dɪˈmɪnɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）减少；轻视，贬低</td><td>diminish in importance<br>重要性下降</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His influence diminished steadily after he left the board.  他离开董事会后影响力持续减弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 discount</td><td>/dɪˈskaʊnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>低估，不予重视；折扣</td><td>discount his warnings<br>对他的警告不予重视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Managers discounted the early warnings about the leaking roof.  管理者对屋顶漏水的早期警告不予重视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reduce</td><td>/rəˈduːs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减少，降低</td><td>reduce the cost sharply<br>大幅降低成本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New machines reduced the factory's energy use by half.  新机器把工厂的能耗降低了一半。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 underestimate</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈestɪmeɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>低估，估计不足</td><td>underestimate the difficulty<br>低估难度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Never underestimate how long these repairs will actually take.  千万不要低估这些修理实际需要的时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 underrate</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>低估，看轻</td><td>underrate an opponent<br>轻视对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics badly underrated her first novel at the time.  评论界当时严重低估了她的第一部小说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undervalue</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈvæljuː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>低估价值，看轻</td><td>undervalue her contribution<br>低估她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company undervalued its most experienced engineers for years.  公司多年来低估了最有经验的工程师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inflate</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使膨胀；夸大</td><td>inflate the figures<br>夸大数字</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company inflated its sales figures to impress investors.  公司夸大销售数字以取悦投资者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 magnify</td><td>/ˈmæɡnəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放大；夸大</td><td>magnify the problem<br>夸大问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lens magnifies the specimen four hundred times.  这个镜头把样本放大四百倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mint</b></td><td>/mɪnt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无损坏的</td><td>in mint condition<br>完好如新</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He keeps his old comic books in mint condition inside sealed plastic sleeves.  他把旧漫画书装在密封塑料套里，保持完好如新。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intact</td><td>/ɪnˈtækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>完好无损的</td><td>remain intact<br>保持完好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Remarkably, the vase survived the earthquake completely intact.  令人惊讶的是，这只花瓶在地震中完好无损。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perfect</td><td>/ˈpɜːrfɪkt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>完美的；使完善</td><td>a perfect score<br>满分成绩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent years trying to perfect his technique on the cello.  他花了数年时间钻研以完善自己的大提琴技巧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pristine</td><td>/prɪˈstiːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崭新的；纯净的；原始的</td><td>pristine white snow<br>洁白无垢的雪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabin sat beside a pristine lake untouched by development.  那间小屋坐落在一个未受开发影响的纯净湖泊旁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unmarred</del></td><td>/ʌnˈmɑːrd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未受损的，完好无瑕的</td><td>an unmarred surface<br>完好无损的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old table is unmarred by scratches or dark stains.  这张旧桌子没有刮痕或污渍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virginal</td><td>/ˈvɜːrdʒənəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未经触碰的，纯净的</td><td>a virginal stretch of beach<br>一段未经开发的海滩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They camped beside a virginal stretch of white beach.  他们在一段未经开发的白色沙滩旁扎营。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impaired</td><td>/ɪmˈperd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>受损的，功能减弱的</td><td>impaired hearing<br>听力受损</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His impaired eyesight forced him to stop driving at night.  视力受损使他不得不放弃夜间开车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 damaged</td><td>/ˈdæmədʒd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>损坏的，受损的</td><td>damaged goods<br>损坏的货物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The store sells damaged books at half the usual price.  这家店以平常一半的价格出售受损的书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>minuscule</b></td><td>/ˈmɪnəskjuːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的</td><td>a minuscule amount<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A minuscule crack in the pipe caused the entire basement to flood overnight.  管道上一道极小的裂缝一夜之间让整个地下室被淹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atomic</td><td>/əˈtɑːmɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极微小的；原子的</td><td>atomic particles of dust<br>微如原子的灰尘颗粒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Under the lens we saw almost atomic specks of pollen.  在镜头下我们看到几乎微如原子的花粉粒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infinitesimal</td><td>/ˌɪnfɪnɪˈtesɪməl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极微小的，无穷小的</td><td>an infinitesimal chance<br>微乎其微的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk of infection is infinitesimal if the tools are sterile.  只要器械无菌，感染风险微乎其微。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 microscopic</td><td>/ˌmaɪkrəˈskɑːpɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微小的；显微镜下的</td><td>microscopic dust particles<br>微小的尘埃颗粒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scientists found microscopic cracks in the metal beam.  科学家在金属梁中发现了极微小的裂缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miniature</td><td>/ˈmɪniətʃər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>微型的；微型画</td><td>a miniature version<br>微缩版本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum displays a miniature model of the whole city.  博物馆展出了整座城市的微缩模型。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minute</td><td>/maɪˈnjuːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的；细致的</td><td>a minute quantity<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even a minute trace of the chemical can spoil the batch.  即使极微量的这种化学品也会毁掉整批产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tiny</td><td>/ˈtaɪni/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微小的</td><td>a tiny fraction<br>极小的一部分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a tiny number of applicants pass the first round.  只有极少数申请者能通过第一轮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 colossal</td><td>/kəˈlɑːsl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a colossal statue<br>巨大的雕像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A colossal statue of the founder stands in the square.  广场上矗立着创始人的巨大雕像。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elephantine</td><td>/ˌelɪˈfæntiːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；笨拙的</td><td>elephantine movements<br>笨重的动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His elephantine movements on stage made the delicate dance look almost comic.  他在台上笨重的动作让这支细腻的舞蹈显得近乎滑稽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enormous</td><td>/ɪˈnɔːrməs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>an enormous amount of data<br>庞大的数据量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project required an enormous amount of money and patience.  这个项目需要巨额资金和极大耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gargantuan</td><td>/ɡɑːrˈɡæntʃuən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a gargantuan appetite<br>巨大的食量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young athlete has a gargantuan appetite after every practice.  这名年轻运动员每次训练后都有巨大的食量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gigantic</td><td>/dʒaɪˈɡæntɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a gigantic stone statue<br>巨大的石像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A gigantic stone statue guards the entrance to the temple.  一座巨大的石像守卫着神庙的入口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 huge</td><td>/ˈhjuːdʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>a huge success<br>巨大的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A huge crowd waited outside the stadium in the rain.  一大群人在雨中等在体育场外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immense</td><td>/ɪˈmens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，极大的</td><td>an immense fortune<br>巨额财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project demanded an immense amount of time and money.  这个项目需要投入巨大的时间和金钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mammoth</td><td>/ˈmæməθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>巨大的；猛犸象</td><td>a mammoth task<br>艰巨的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rebuilding the port after the storm was a mammoth undertaking.  风暴后重建港口是一项巨大的工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prodigious</td><td>/prəˈdɪdʒəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；惊人的，了不起的</td><td>a prodigious memory<br>惊人的记忆力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist has a prodigious memory for long and complicated scores.  这位钢琴家对冗长复杂的乐谱有惊人的记忆力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>minute</b></td><td>/maɪˈnjuːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的；细致的</td><td>a minute quantity<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even a minute trace of the chemical can spoil the batch.  即使极微量的这种化学品也会毁掉整批产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circumstantial</td><td>/ˌsɜːrkəmˈstænʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>详细的；根据推断的</td><td>a circumstantial account<br>详尽的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave a long, circumstantial description of that evening.  证人对那个晚上作了长篇详尽的描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elaborate</td><td>/ɪˈlæbəreɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>详细阐述；精心制作的</td><td>elaborate on a point<br>详述某一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Could you elaborate on your last point a little?  你能就最后一点再详细说明一下吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 full</td><td>/ˈfʊl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>完整的，充满的</td><td>a full account<br>完整的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a full explanation of the accident to police.  他向警方对事故作了完整说明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 particular</td><td>/pərˈtɪkjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>详细的；特定的；讲究的</td><td>a particular account of events<br>对事件的详细说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a particular account of every repair the house needed.  他详细说明了房子所需的每一项修缮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 particularized</td><td>/pərˈtɪkjələraɪzd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>详述的，逐项列明的</td><td>a particularized cost estimate<br>逐项列明的成本估算</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The auditor demanded a particularized record of every payment.  审计员要求提供每笔付款的逐项记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thorough</td><td>/ˈθɜːroʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彻底的，详尽的</td><td>a thorough investigation<br>彻底的调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors carried out a thorough search of every storage room.  检查员对每间储藏室都做了彻底搜查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frivolous</td><td>/ˈfrɪvələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的；无关紧要的</td><td>a frivolous remark<br>轻率无谓的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge dismissed the frivolous lawsuit within a single morning.  法官在一个上午之内就驳回了这起无理的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incidental</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪˈdentəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>偶然的；附带的</td><td>incidental expenses<br>杂项开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The damage was incidental to the main purpose of the test.  这种损坏只是测试主要目的之外的附带结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 little</td><td>/ˈlɪtəl/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微小的，少的</td><td>a little detail<br>一个小细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She noticed one little detail that everyone else had missed.  她注意到一个别人都漏掉的小细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 minor</td><td>/ˈmaɪnər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>较小的，不重要的</td><td>a minor problem<br>一个小问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only minor repairs were needed after the small accident.  小事故后只需要一些小修理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negligible</td><td>/ˈneɡlədʒəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的，可忽略的</td><td>a negligible difference<br>可忽略的差异</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extra cost is negligible compared with the savings.  与节省的钱相比，额外成本微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nugatory</del></td><td>/ˈnuːɡətɔːri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的</td><td>a nugatory objection<br>无关紧要的反对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court dismissed his nugatory objection and proceeded with the hearing.  法庭驳回了他无关紧要的反对，继续进行听证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brief</td><td>/ˈbriːf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简短的，简洁的</td><td>a brief summary<br>简短的总结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a brief account of the accident to the police.  他向警方简短叙述了事故经过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compendious</td><td>/kəmˈpɛndiəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明扼要的</td><td>a compendious reference guide<br>简明扼要的参考手册</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wrote a compendious history of the city in ninety pages.  他用九十页写成了一部简明的城市史。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concise</td><td>/kənˈsaɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a concise explanation<br>简明的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep your answer concise; the interviewer has only ten minutes.  回答要简洁，面试官只有十分钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 succinct</td><td>/səkˈsɪŋkt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简明的，简洁的</td><td>a succinct summary<br>简洁的总结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her succinct summary covered the entire report in three short paragraphs.  她简洁的总结用三小段概括了整份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 summary</td><td>/ˈsʌməri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>概括的，简略的；摘要</td><td>a summary of the report<br>报告的摘要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please attach a one-page summary to the front of the proposal.  请在提案前面附上一页摘要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 terse</td><td>/tɜːrs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁的，简明的</td><td>a terse reply<br>简洁的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His terse note said only that the deal had collapsed.  他简短的字条只说交易破裂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 critical</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>关键的；批评的</td><td>a critical moment<br>关键时刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water supply is critical to the survival of this town.  供水对这座小镇的存续至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 crucial</td><td>/ˈkruːʃl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常重要的，决定性的</td><td>a crucial decision<br>关键的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The next two weeks will be crucial to the project's survival.  接下来两周对这个项目的存亡至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vital</td><td>/ˈvaɪtəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满活力的；极重要的</td><td>full of vital energy<br>充满生命活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular exercise keeps older people vital and mentally alert.  经常锻炼使老年人保持活力和头脑清醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weighty</td><td>/ˈweɪti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的；沉重的</td><td>weighty matters<br>重大的事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The elders debated weighty questions of land and water.  长者们讨论着土地与水源的重大问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>minutiae</b></td><td>/mɪˈnuːʃiiː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>次要的细节，小事</td><td>the minutiae of daily life<br>日常生活的琐事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He worries about the minutiae of daily life and forgets the larger goals.  他纠结于日常生活的琐事，却忘了更大的目标。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 triviality</td><td>/ˌtrɪviˈæləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>琐事；微不足道</td><td>argue over trivialities<br>为琐事争吵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long meeting sank into arguments about pure trivialities.  漫长的会议陷入了对纯粹琐事的争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gist</td><td>/dʒɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>要点</td><td>get the gist of<br>抓住…的要点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She summarized the gist of the long report in only three sentences.  她只用三句话就概括了那份长报告的要点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mirage</b></td><td>/məˈrɑːʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>海市蜃楼；幻景；妄想</td><td>chase a mirage<br>追逐幻影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shimmering lake ahead turned out to be only a mirage.  前方波光粼粼的湖泊原来只是海市蜃楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chimera</td><td>/kaɪˈmɪrə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>虚构的事物，幻觉</td><td>a mere chimera<br>纯粹的幻想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lasting peace seemed a mere chimera to the exhausted soldiers.  对疲惫的士兵来说，持久和平仿佛只是幻想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delusion</td><td>/dɪˈluːʒn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>错觉，妄想</td><td>labor under a delusion<br>抱着错误的幻想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He clung to the delusion that the company would soon rehire him.  他固执地抱着公司很快会重新雇用他的妄想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hallucination</td><td>/həˌluːsɪˈneɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幻觉</td><td>suffer hallucinations<br>出现幻觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The high fever gave him vivid hallucinations that lasted through the night.  高烧让他产生了持续整夜的鲜明幻觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 illusion</td><td>/ɪˈluːʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>错觉，幻想</td><td>an optical illusion<br>视觉错觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mirrors create the illusion that the room is twice as large.  镜子造成了房间大了一倍的错觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phantom</td><td>/ˈfæntəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>幻象，幻影；虚幻的</td><td>a phantom of the past<br>过往的幻影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He kept chasing the phantom of an easy fortune.  他一直追逐轻松发财的幻影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vision</td><td>/ˈvɪʒən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>幻象，梦幻；视力；远见</td><td>have a vision of<br>看到…的幻象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The saint claimed to have seen a vision of a burning city.  这位圣徒声称看到过一座燃烧城市的幻象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reality</td><td>/riˈæləti/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>现实，真实</td><td>face reality<br>面对现实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later he must face the reality of his debts.  他迟早得面对自己债务的现实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mire</b></td><td>/ˈmaɪər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>泥沼；泥潭；困境</td><td>a mire of debt<br>债务的泥潭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company sank into a mire of debt from which it never escaped.  公司陷入债务的泥潭，再也没能脱身。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dilemma</td><td>/dɪˈlemə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，两难处境</td><td>face a painful dilemma<br>面临痛苦的两难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced the dilemma of staying home or taking the job.  她面临留在家里还是接受工作的两难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hole</td><td>/ˈhoʊl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，窘境；洞</td><td>get into a hole<br>陷入窘境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bad loans put the small company in a very deep hole.  不良贷款让这家小公司陷入很深的困境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impasse</td><td>/ˈɪmpæs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>僵局，绝境</td><td>reach an impasse<br>陷入僵局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The talks reached an impasse over pay and working hours.  谈判在薪酬和工时上陷入僵局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jam</td><td>/ˈdʒæm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，窘境；堵塞</td><td>be in a real jam<br>处境十分为难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We were in a real jam when the car broke down.  车坏了的时候我们真的很为难。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pickle</td><td>/ˈpɪkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，麻烦；腌菜</td><td>in a pretty pickle<br>处于相当尴尬的境地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Losing his passport left him in a nasty pickle abroad.  丢了护照让他在国外陷入麻烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quagmire</td><td>/ˈkwæɡmaɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，泥潭</td><td>a legal quagmire<br>法律上的泥潭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company slowly sank into a quagmire of lawsuits.  公司慢慢陷入了诉讼的泥潭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 rattrap</del></td><td>/ˈrættræp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境，进退无路之地</td><td>caught in a rattrap<br>陷在进退无路的困境中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old contract turned out to be a rattrap for both sides.  那份旧合同结果对双方都是死局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swamp</td><td>/ˈswɑːmp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困境；沼泽</td><td>a swamp of paperwork<br>文书工作的泥潭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was lost in a swamp of forms and old receipts.  他陷在表格和旧收据的泥潭里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bog</td><td>/ˈbɑːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>泥沼；困境</td><td>a bog of regulations<br>法规的泥沼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan got stuck in a bog of official rules.  这个计划陷在官方规定的泥沼中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 broil</td><td>/ˈbrɔɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>争吵，混乱的局面</td><td>a political broil<br>一场政治纷争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village was caught in a broil over the new road.  村子因新修的路陷入一场纷争。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delay</td><td>/dɪˈleɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>延迟，推迟</td><td>delay the departure<br>推迟出发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy fog delayed the flight by more than four hours.  大雾使航班延误了四个多小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detain</td><td>/dɪˈteɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拘留，扣押；留住</td><td>detain a suspect<br>拘留嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police detained the two men for questioning about the missing car.  警方拘留了这两名男子，就失踪汽车一事问话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entangle</td><td>/ɪnˈtæŋɡl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使缠住，使卷入；使复杂</td><td>entangle the negotiations<br>使谈判变复杂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New objections entangled the negotiations and delayed the deal for weeks.  新的反对意见使谈判陷入纠缠，把交易推迟了数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entrap</td><td>/ɪnˈtræp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>entrap an unwary buyer<br>诱骗不留神的买主</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The con artist entrapped an unwary buyer with a forged certificate.  骗子用一张伪造证书诱骗了一位不留神的买主。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retard</td><td>/rɪˈtɑːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减速，延迟</td><td>retard the growth<br>延缓生长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Language barriers retarded the progress of the peace negotiations.  语言障碍阻碍了和平谈判的进展。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enfranchise</td><td>/ɪnˈfræntʃaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>给予…权利（例如选举权）；解放</td><td>enfranchise women<br>赋予妇女选举权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The amendment enfranchised millions of citizens for the first time.  该修正案首次赋予数百万公民选举权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extricate</td><td>/ˈekstrɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使解脱，救出</td><td>extricate himself from debt<br>使自己脱离债务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took him years to extricate himself from serious debt.  他花了好几年才使自己脱离沉重债务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 free</td><td>/ˈfriː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，解放；自由的</td><td>free the hostages<br>释放人质</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Negotiators finally persuaded the gunmen to free the hostages.  谈判者最终说服枪手释放了人质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 liberate</td><td>/ˈlɪbəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解放，释放</td><td>liberate the city<br>解放这座城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Allied troops liberated the city in the spring of that year.  盟军在那年春天解放了这座城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rescue</td><td>/ˈreskju/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>解救，营救</td><td>rescue the trapped miners<br>营救被困矿工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Helicopters rescued twelve people from the flooded village.  直升机从被淹的村庄救出十二人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mirth</b></td><td>/mɜːrθ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐，欢笑</td><td>a burst of mirth<br>一阵欢笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A burst of mirth from the kitchen told us the party had begun.  厨房里传来一阵欢笑，告诉我们派对已经开始了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cheer</td><td>/ˈtʃɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>欢呼，喝彩；使高兴</td><td>a loud cheer<br>一阵响亮的欢呼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A loud cheer rose from the crowd when the goal was scored.  进球时人群中响起一阵响亮的欢呼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 festivity</td><td>/feˈstɪvəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢庆，喜庆活动</td><td>a night of festivity<br>欢庆的一夜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole street joined the festivity after the harvest.  收获之后整条街都加入了欢庆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaiety</td><td>/ˈɡeɪəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐，愉快</td><td>an air of gaiety<br>欢快的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Music and colored lights filled the square with gaiety.  音乐和彩灯让广场充满欢乐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glee</td><td>/ˈɡliː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢欣，快乐</td><td>rub one's hands in glee<br>高兴地搓着手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children shouted with glee when the snow began.  开始下雪时孩子们欢呼雀跃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 happiness</td><td>/ˈhæpinəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幸福，快乐</td><td>a moment of pure happiness<br>纯粹快乐的一刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small daily habits brought her more happiness than money did.  日常的小习惯给她的快乐比钱更多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hilarity</td><td>/hɪˈlerəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢闹，大笑</td><td>cause general hilarity<br>引起满堂大笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His clumsy dance caused hilarity among all the guests.  他笨拙的舞步引得所有客人大笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 jocundity</del></td><td>/dʒoʊˈkʌndəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐，快活</td><td>the jocundity of the crowd<br>人群的欢快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old inn was famous for its noisy jocundity.  这家老客栈以喧闹的欢快闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 joviality</td><td>/ˌdʒoʊviˈæləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>快活，兴高采烈</td><td>his easy joviality<br>他随和的快活劲儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His joviality at dinner put every guest at ease.  他在饭桌上的快活让每位客人都放松了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lightheartedness</td><td>/ˌlaɪtˈhɑrtɪdnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>轻松愉快，无忧无虑</td><td>a mood of lightheartedness<br>轻松愉快的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a lightheartedness in the office before the holiday.  假期前办公室里气氛轻松愉快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dejection</td><td>/dɪˈdʒɛkʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>沮丧，忧郁</td><td>a look of dejection<br>沮丧的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat in silent dejection after the results came out.  成绩出来后他沮丧地默默坐着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 depression</td><td>/dɪˈpreʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>沮丧，抑郁；萧条</td><td>sink into deep depression<br>陷入深度抑郁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After losing his job he slid into a long depression.  失业之后，他陷入了长久的抑郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 desolation</td><td>/ˌdesəˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>荒凉，凄凉；悲痛</td><td>a scene of desolation<br>一片荒凉的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty village left an impression of complete desolation.  空无一人的村子给人彻底荒凉之感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 despondence</del></td><td>/dɪˈspɑːndəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>沮丧，意气消沉</td><td>sink into despondence<br>陷入沮丧消沉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Months without work drove him into deep despondence.  数月失业让他陷入深深的沮丧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doldrums</td><td>/ˈdoʊldrəmz/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>低迷，停滞</td><td>in the doldrums<br>处于低迷状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The housing market has been in the doldrums all year.  房地产市场整年都处于低迷状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 melancholy</td><td>/ˈmelənkɑːli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>忧郁，伤感</td><td>a mood of quiet melancholy<br>淡淡的忧郁情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Autumn rain always fills him with a vague melancholy.  秋雨总让他心里泛起一种莫名的忧郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 oppression</td><td>/əˈpreʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>压抑，郁闷；压迫</td><td>a feeling of oppression<br>一种压抑感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The low grey sky gave her a sense of oppression.  低垂的灰色天空让她感到压抑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>misanthropic</b></td><td>/ˌmɪsənˈθrɑpɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>厌恶人类的，愤世嫉俗的</td><td>a misanthropic recluse<br>厌世的隐居者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The misanthropic old writer refused every invitation he received.  这位厌世的老作家拒绝了他收到的每份邀请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antisocial</td><td>/ˌæntɪˈsoʊʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反社会的，不合群的</td><td>antisocial behavior<br>反社会的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud parties every single night are simply antisocial behavior.  每晚都吵闹的聚会纯属妨害他人的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cynical</td><td>/ˈsɪnɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愤世嫉俗的，怀疑一切的</td><td>a cynical remark<br>愤世嫉俗的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years in politics made him cynical about every promise.  多年从政让他对任何承诺都持怀疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pessimistic</td><td>/ˌpesɪˈmɪstɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲观的</td><td>a pessimistic outlook<br>悲观的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained pessimistic about the company's chances of survival.  他对公司生存的机会仍然持悲观态度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 philanthropic</td><td>/ˌfɪlənˈθrɑːpɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>博爱的，为他人着想的</td><td>philanthropic donations<br>慈善捐赠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Philanthropic donations paid for the new hospital wing for sick children.  慈善捐赠支付了收治病童的医院新翼楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>misbehaving</b></td><td>/ˌmɪsbɪˈheɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>行为不端的，捣乱的</td><td>misbehaving students<br>捣乱的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher quietly separated the two misbehaving boys.  老师悄悄把那两个捣乱的男孩分开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoying</td><td>/əˈnɔɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>讨厌的，恼人的</td><td>an annoying habit<br>恼人的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tapping his pen was easily his most annoying habit.  敲笔无疑是他最恼人的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 errant</td><td>/ˈerənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>误入歧途的，犯错误的</td><td>an errant husband<br>出轨的丈夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The errant student was suspended after ignoring three separate warnings.  这名行为不端的学生在忽视三次警告后被停学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impish</td><td>/ˈɪmpɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽皮的，淘气的</td><td>an impish grin<br>顽皮的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impish grin and hid the last cookie.  她顽皮地一笑，把最后一块饼干藏了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mischievous</td><td>/ˈmɪstʃɪvəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的；调皮的</td><td>a mischievous rumor<br>有害的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mischievous rumor damaged her reputation for several years.  这个有害的谣言损害了她多年的名誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 naughty</td><td>/ˈnɔːti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>淘气的，不听话的</td><td>a naughty child<br>淘气的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The naughty puppy chewed both of my new shoes.  这只淘气的小狗把我两只新鞋都咬了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 playful</td><td>/ˈpleɪfl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱开玩笑的，好打闹的</td><td>a playful tone<br>玩闹的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her playful tone made it clear that she was only teasing him.  她玩闹的语气清楚表明她只是在逗他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wicked</td><td>/ˈwɪkɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的</td><td>a wicked scheme<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wicked scheme to seize the estate was uncovered by the lawyer.  律师揭穿了一个夺取遗产的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decorous</td><td>/ˈdekərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体端正的</td><td>decorous behavior at dinner<br>晚餐时得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guests kept a decorous silence while the old bishop said grace.  老主教做餐前祷告时，客人们保持着得体的安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 urbane</td><td>/ərˈbeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彬彬有礼的，文雅的</td><td>an urbane host<br>文雅的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our urbane host guided the conversation with easy wit and grace.  我们文雅的主人以轻松的机智和风度引导着谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>mischievous</b></td><td>/ˈmɪstʃɪvəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的；调皮的</td><td>a mischievous rumor<br>有害的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mischievous rumor damaged her reputation for several years.  这个有害的谣言损害了她多年的名誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoying</td><td>/əˈnɔɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>讨厌的，恼人的</td><td>an annoying habit<br>恼人的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tapping his pen was easily his most annoying habit.  敲笔无疑是他最恼人的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 errant</td><td>/ˈerənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>误入歧途的，犯错误的</td><td>an errant husband<br>出轨的丈夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The errant student was suspended after ignoring three separate warnings.  这名行为不端的学生在忽视三次警告后被停学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impish</td><td>/ˈɪmpɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽皮的，淘气的</td><td>an impish grin<br>顽皮的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impish grin and hid the last cookie.  她顽皮地一笑，把最后一块饼干藏了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misbehaving</td><td>/ˌmɪsbɪˈheɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>行为不端的，捣乱的</td><td>misbehaving students<br>捣乱的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher quietly separated the two misbehaving boys.  老师悄悄把那两个捣乱的男孩分开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 naughty</td><td>/ˈnɔːti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>淘气的，不听话的</td><td>a naughty child<br>淘气的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The naughty puppy chewed both of my new shoes.  这只淘气的小狗把我两只新鞋都咬了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 playful</td><td>/ˈpleɪfl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱开玩笑的，好打闹的</td><td>a playful tone<br>玩闹的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her playful tone made it clear that she was only teasing him.  她玩闹的语气清楚表明她只是在逗他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wicked</td><td>/ˈwɪkɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的</td><td>a wicked scheme<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wicked scheme to seize the estate was uncovered by the lawyer.  律师揭穿了一个夺取遗产的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decorous</td><td>/ˈdekərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体端正的</td><td>decorous behavior at dinner<br>晚餐时得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guests kept a decorous silence while the old bishop said grace.  老主教做餐前祷告时，客人们保持着得体的安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 urbane</td><td>/ərˈbeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彬彬有礼的，文雅的</td><td>an urbane host<br>文雅的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our urbane host guided the conversation with easy wit and grace.  我们文雅的主人以轻松的机智和风度引导着谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>misconstrue</b></td><td>/ˌmɪskənˈstruː/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误解，曲解</td><td>misconstrue her silence<br>误解她的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Outsiders often misconstrue her quiet manner as cold arrogance.  局外人常把她安静的举止曲解为冷漠傲慢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garble</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>曲解，篡改，混淆（以至使无法理解）</td><td>garble the message<br>曲解这条消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bad connection garbled the message beyond all understanding.  信号不好把这条消息混淆得完全无法理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misapprehend</del></td><td>/ˌmɪsæprɪˈhɛnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误解，误会</td><td>misapprehend the question<br>误解了这个问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She misapprehended his joke and left the room angry.  她误解了他的玩笑，生气地离开了房间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misinterpret</td><td>/ˌmɪsɪnˈtɜːrprɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误解，误读</td><td>misinterpret the data<br>误读数据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers often misinterpret irony as sincere praise.  读者常把讽刺误解为真诚的赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 misperceive</del></td><td>/ˌmɪspərˈsiːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>错误地理解，误认</td><td>misperceive the situation<br>对形势判断错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors misperceived the risk and lost a great deal.  投资者错判了风险，损失惨重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misread</td><td>/ˌmɪsˈriːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误解，看错</td><td>misread the signs<br>看错了迹象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He misread her polite tone as real enthusiasm.  他把她客气的语气误读成真正的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misrepresent</td><td>/ˌmɪsˌreprɪˈzent/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误传，篡改</td><td>misrepresent the facts<br>歪曲事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics accused the report of misrepresenting the facts to favor one side.  批评者指责这份报告歪曲事实以偏向一方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mistake</td><td>/mɪˈsteɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>错误，过失；误解</td><td>make a mistake<br>犯错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone makes mistakes, but few admit them so quickly.  人人都会犯错，但很少有人这么快承认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appreciate</td><td>/əˈpriːʃieɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>增值，涨价；欣赏</td><td>appreciate in value<br>价值上涨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old farmhouse has appreciated considerably over the last decade.  这座老农舍在过去十年里大幅增值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apprehend</td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>逮捕；理解</td><td>apprehend the suspect<br>逮捕嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police apprehended the thief two streets from the shop.  警方在离商店两条街的地方逮住了小偷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 catch</td><td>/ˈkætʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>染上（疾病）；抓住</td><td>catch a cold<br>感冒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You will catch a cold if you go out with wet hair.  你要是湿着头发出门就会感冒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comprehend</td><td>/ˌkɑːmprɪˈhend/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>理解，了解；包括，包含</td><td>comprehend the full report<br>理解整份报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few readers can comprehend the full report without expert help.  很少有读者能在没有专家帮助时理解整份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fathom</td><td>/ˈfæðəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底理解，弄懂</td><td>fathom his motives<br>弄懂他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could fathom his motives for leaving so suddenly.  没人能弄懂他如此突然离开的动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grasp</td><td>/ˈɡræsp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>抓紧，握牢；理解</td><td>grasp the handle<br>抓住把手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grasped the rope tightly and pulled herself up.  她紧抓绳子，把自己拉了上去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perceive</td><td>/pərˈsiːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>察觉，理解</td><td>perceive a subtle change<br>察觉细微的变化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She perceived a subtle change in his tone during dinner.  晚餐时她察觉到他语气的微妙变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 savvy</td><td>/ˈsævi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>老练，机智；有见识的，精明能干的</td><td>political savvy<br>政治上的老练</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her political savvy helped the bill pass with almost no opposition.  她政治上的老练使那项议案几乎无异议通过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seize</td><td>/ˈsiːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>领会，抓住（要点）</td><td>seize the main point<br>抓住要点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good students quickly seize the main point of a difficult lecture.  好学生能迅速抓住难懂讲座的要点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 understand</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈstænd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>理解，懂得</td><td>understand the question<br>理解问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I finally understand why the experiment kept failing.  我终于理解实验为何一直失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>miscreant</b></td><td>/ˈmɪskriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶棍，罪犯；邪恶的</td><td>punish the miscreants<br>惩罚这些恶棍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge promised to punish the miscreants who had vandalized the village school.  法官承诺惩罚那些破坏村校的恶棍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brute</td><td>/ˈbruːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>残暴之人，野蛮人</td><td>a drunken brute<br>醉酒的野蛮之人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge called him a brute who terrorized his neighbors.  法官称他是恐吓邻居的野蛮之徒。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 culprit</td><td>/ˈkʌlprɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>罪犯，肇事者；起因</td><td>find the real culprit<br>找出真正的肇事者</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 devil</td><td>/ˈdevəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>魔鬼，恶魔；坏家伙</td><td>a poor devil<br>可怜的家伙</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 offender</td><td>/əˈfendər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>犯罪者，违法者</td><td>a first-time offender<br>初犯者</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rascal</td><td>/ˈræskəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无赖，流氓；小淘气</td><td>a little rascal<br>小淘气鬼</td></tr>
