<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bottom</td><td>/ˈbɑːtəm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>底部，最低点</td><td>at the bottom<br>在底部</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their team finished at the bottom of the league again.  他们的球队又一次排在联赛末位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foot</td><td>/ˈfʊt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>底部，最下端</td><td>at the foot of the hill<br>在山脚下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A small inn stands at the foot of the mountain.  山脚下有一家小旅店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acme</td><td>/ˈækmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，极点</td><td>the acme of perfection<br>完美的极致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That performance marked the acme of her long career.  那场演出标志着她漫长职业生涯的顶峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apex</td><td>/ˈeɪˌpeks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，最高点</td><td>the apex of his career<br>他事业的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At the apex of her fame she suddenly left the stage.  在名声的顶峰，她突然离开了舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 climax</td><td>/ˈklaɪˌmæks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>高潮，顶点</td><td>the climax of the story<br>故事的高潮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film builds slowly toward a violent and unexpected climax.  影片缓慢推向一个暴烈而意外的高潮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meridian</td><td>/məˈrɪdiən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，全盛期；子午线</td><td>at the meridian of his career<br>在他事业顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The composer died at the meridian of his creative powers.  这位作曲家在创作力顶峰时去世。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peak</td><td>/ˈpiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>顶峰，最高点</td><td>at the peak of<br>在…的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales peaked in December and then fell sharply in January.  销售在十二月达到顶峰，一月急剧下滑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pinnacle</td><td>/ˈpɪnəkl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶峰，巅峰</td><td>the pinnacle of success<br>成功的顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At thirty she had reached the pinnacle of her profession.  三十岁时她已达到职业生涯的巅峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 summit</td><td>/ˈsʌmɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点</td><td>reach the summit<br>登上顶峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The climbers reached the summit just before the storm closed in.  登山者在风暴逼近前登上顶峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 top</td><td>/ˈtɑːp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>顶点，最高处</td><td>at the top of the list<br>在名单最上方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her name sat at the top of the shortlist.  她的名字排在候选名单最上方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 zenith</td><td>/ˈzenɪθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>最高点，巅峰</td><td>at the zenith of<br>在…的巅峰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empire stood at the zenith of its power around that year.  大约在那一年，这个帝国正处于国力的巅峰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>naive</b></td><td>/naɪˈiːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真的，幼稚的</td><td>naive assumptions<br>天真的假设</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was naive to expect honesty from someone with that record.  指望有那种记录的人诚实太天真了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 artless</td><td>/ˈɑrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏技巧的，笨拙的</td><td>an artless imitation<br>拙劣的模仿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting was an artless copy of a famous portrait.  这幅画是对一幅名作的拙劣临摹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guileless</td><td>/ˈɡaɪlləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单纯的，不狡诈的</td><td>a guileless smile<br>天真无邪的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her guileless smile made everyone in the room trust her immediately.  她天真无邪的笑容让屋里所有人立刻信任她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ingenuous</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒenjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>天真淳朴的</td><td>an ingenuous young student<br>天真淳朴的年轻学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ingenuous questions about the city amused the veteran taxi driver greatly.  他关于这座城市的天真提问让老出租车司机大为好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 innocent</td><td>/ˈɪnəsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，无恶意的；无辜的</td><td>an innocent remark<br>无恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What began as an innocent joke ended in a serious quarrel.  一句本无恶意的玩笑最后引发了激烈争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 natural</td><td>/ˈnætʃərəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自然的，正常的</td><td>a natural reaction<br>自然的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is natural to feel nervous before a big exam.  大考前紧张是很自然的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unaffected</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈfektɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不受影响的</td><td>unaffected by the crisis<br>未受危机影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Northern farms remained largely unaffected by the drought.  北方的农场基本未受这场旱灾影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsophisticated</td><td>/ˌʌnsəˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单纯的，不老练的</td><td>an unsophisticated buyer<br>不老练的买家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unsophisticated investors are often the first to lose their money.  不老练的投资者往往最先赔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sophisticated</td><td>/səˈfɪstɪkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>复杂精密的；老练的</td><td>a sophisticated system<br>复杂精密的系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lab uses highly sophisticated equipment to detect the particles.  实验室用极为精密的设备探测这些粒子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 worldly</td><td>/ˈwɜːrldli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>世间的，世俗的；老练的</td><td>worldly possessions<br>世俗财产</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave away all his worldly possessions and entered the monastery.  他捐出全部世俗财产，进了修道院。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>narcissistic</b></td><td>/ˌnɑːrsɪˈsɪstɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自恋的</td><td>narcissistic self-regard<br>自恋的自我关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His narcissistic self-regard made honest conversation with him nearly impossible.  他自恋的自我关注让人几乎无法与他坦诚交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 egotistical</td><td>/ˌiːɡəˈtɪstɪkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自我中心的，自负的</td><td>an egotistical actor<br>自负的演员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The egotistical actor demanded a bigger dressing room.  这位自负的演员要求更大的化妆间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-absorbed</td><td>/ˌsɛlf əbˈsɔrbd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>只顾自己的，自我专注的</td><td>too self-absorbed to notice<br>太自我而未察觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too self-absorbed to notice his friend's distress.  他太自我，没注意到朋友的痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-centered</td><td>/ˌselfˈsentərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>自我中心的，自私的</td><td>a self-centered child<br>自我中心的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is so self-centered that he never asks how others feel.  他极其自我中心，从不问别人的感受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-infatuated</td><td>/ˌsɛlf ɪnˈfætʃueɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自我迷恋的</td><td>a self-infatuated poet<br>自我迷恋的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The self-infatuated poet quoted himself all evening.  这位自恋的诗人整晚都在引用自己。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-obsessed</td><td>/ˌsɛlf əbˈsɛst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自我着迷的，只想着自己的</td><td>a self-obsessed celebrity<br>只想着自己的名人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her diary reads like the work of a self-obsessed teenager.  她的日记读来像自我着迷的少年所写。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 self-preoccupied</td><td>/ˌsɛlf priˈɑkjupaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>全神贯注于自身的</td><td>a self-preoccupied manner<br>只顾自己的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He listened in a self-preoccupied way, waiting to speak.  他心里只顾自己地听着，只等开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 altruistic</td><td>/ˌæltruˈɪstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无私的，利他的</td><td>altruistic motives<br>利他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her altruistic work in the clinic earned wide public respect.  她在诊所无私的工作赢得了公众广泛尊敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 self-forgetting</td><td>/ˌsɛlf fərˈɡɛtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>忘我的，无私的</td><td>self-forgetting devotion<br>忘我的奉献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her self-forgetting care for the patients moved the whole ward.  她对病人忘我的照护感动了整个病房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 selfless</td><td>/ˈselfləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无私的，不自私的</td><td>selfless devotion to others<br>对他人的无私奉献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors praised her selfless care for the elderly couple.  邻居们称赞她对那对老夫妇的无私照料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unselfish</td><td>/ʌnˈselfɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无私的，不自私的</td><td>unselfish devotion<br>无私的奉献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unselfish passing made the whole team better.  他无私的传球让整支球队更出色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nascent</b></td><td>/ˈnæsnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新生的</td><td>a nascent industry<br>新兴的行业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Government loans helped the nascent solar industry survive its first decade.  政府贷款帮助新兴的太阳能产业度过了最初十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beginning</td><td>/bɪˈɡɪnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>最初的，开端的；开始</td><td>the beginning stages<br>起始阶段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We are still at the beginning of the investigation.  我们仍处于调查的开端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emerging</td><td>/ɪˈmɜːrdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新兴的，正出现的</td><td>an emerging market<br>新兴市场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm shifted its attention to emerging markets in Asia.  该公司把注意力转向亚洲新兴市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inceptive</del></td><td>/ɪnˈseptɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>开始的，起始的</td><td>an inceptive phase<br>起始阶段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project is still in its inceptive stage.  该项目仍处于起始阶段。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inchoate</td><td>/ɪnˈkoʊət/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>新生的，才开始的</td><td>inchoate ideas<br>尚未成形的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His inchoate plans changed every time he described them to us.  他那些尚未成形的计划每次向我们描述时都变了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incipient</td><td>/ɪnˈsɪpiənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>起初的，初现的</td><td>incipient signs of decay<br>初现的衰败迹象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dentist spotted incipient signs of decay in two of the teeth.  牙医发现两颗牙齿上有初现的蛀坏迹象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 initial</td><td>/ˌɪˈnɪʃəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>最初的，开始的</td><td>the initial reaction<br>最初的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our initial estimate turned out to be far too low.  我们最初的估计结果远远偏低。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 introductory</td><td>/ˌɪntrəˈdʌktəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>入门的，初步的</td><td>an introductory course<br>入门课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She signed up for an introductory course in statistics.  她报名了统计学入门课程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 full-blown</td><td>/ˌfʊlˈbloʊn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>完全成熟的，全面爆发的</td><td>a full-blown crisis<br>全面爆发的危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A minor dispute grew into a full-blown lawsuit.  一场小争执演变成全面的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 full-fledged</td><td>/ˌfʊl ˈfledʒd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>完全成熟的，羽翼已成的</td><td>a full-fledged member<br>正式成员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After three years she became a full-fledged engineer.  三年后她成为一名完全合格的工程师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mature</td><td>/məˈtʃʊr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>成熟的；（使）成熟</td><td>a mature judgment<br>成熟的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seems remarkably mature for a boy of fifteen.  对一个十五岁男孩来说他显得格外成熟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ripe</td><td>/ˈraɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>成熟的，熟透的；时机成熟的</td><td>ripe for change<br>变革时机成熟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaches are finally ripe enough to pick and eat.  这些桃子终于熟到可以采下来吃了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>natty</b></td><td>/ˈnæti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>整洁的，时髦的</td><td>a natty blue suit<br>时髦的蓝西装</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at the reception in a natty blue suit and polished shoes.  他穿着时髦的蓝西装和擦亮的皮鞋来到招待会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dapper</td><td>/ˈdæpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣冠整洁的</td><td>a dapper little man<br>衣着整洁的小个子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dapper old gentleman in a gray suit greeted us at the door.  一位身穿灰色西装、衣冠整洁的老先生在门口迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smart</td><td>/ˈsmɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的；时髦的</td><td>a smart move<br>明智之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Buying the tickets early was a very smart decision.  提前买票是个非常明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spruce</td><td>/spruːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>整洁漂亮的；打扮整洁</td><td>look neat and spruce<br>显得整洁利落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old waiter always looked spruce despite his worn uniform.  尽管制服磨旧，这位老侍者总是显得整洁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 frowsy</del></td><td>/ˈfraʊzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的</td><td>a frowsy old coat<br>邋遢的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked frowsy after sleeping two nights on the train.  在火车上睡了两夜后，他显得很邋遢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sloppy</td><td>/ˈslɑːpi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的；混乱的</td><td>sloppy handwriting<br>潦草的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sloppy handwriting made the exam answers almost impossible to read.  他潦草的字迹让考卷答案几乎无法辨认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slovenly</td><td>/ˈslʌvnli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的</td><td>slovenly appearance<br>邋遢的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His slovenly desk made a bad impression on every visitor.  他邋遢的办公桌给每位来访者留下坏印象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>nauseate</b></del></td><td>/ˈnɔːzieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使恶心；使厌恶</td><td>nauseate the audience<br>使观众反感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The graphic descriptions in the film nauseated much of the audience.  影片中露骨的描写让大部分观众感到恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disgust</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使厌恶；厌恶</td><td>disgust even his allies<br>连他的盟友也感到厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The state of the kitchen disgusted the health inspector.  厨房的状况让卫生检查员十分厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repel</td><td>/rɪˈpel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>击退，驱退；使厌恶</td><td>repel the attackers<br>击退进攻者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The greasy smell of the kitchen seemed to repel every customer who entered.  厨房油腻的气味似乎让每个进来的顾客都感到厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repulse</td><td>/rɪˈpʌls/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使厌恶，排斥</td><td>repulsed by the smell<br>被那气味恶心到</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sight of the rotting fruit repulsed everyone standing near the crate.  腐烂水果的样子让站在箱子旁的每个人都感到恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revolt</td><td>/rɪˈvoʊlt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使厌恶，使反感</td><td>revolted by the smell<br>被气味恶心到</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of the spoiled milk revolted everyone in the small kitchen.  变质牛奶的气味让狭小厨房里的每个人都反胃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sicken</td><td>/ˈsɪkən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>生病，患病；使厌恶</td><td>sicken and die<br>患病死去</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many of the sheep sickened after drinking from the polluted stream.  许多羊喝了污染的溪水后患病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delight</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使高兴，使愉快；愉悦</td><td>delight the audience<br>使观众高兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist delighted the audience with a lively encore.  这位年轻钢琴家用一首活泼的加演曲取悦了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nautical</b></td><td>/ˈnɔːtɪkl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>航海的</td><td>nautical charts<br>航海图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain spread the old nautical charts across the table.  船长把旧航海图铺在桌上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 marine</td><td>/məˈriːn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>海洋的，海事的</td><td>marine life<br>海洋生物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warming water is changing marine life along this coast.  海水变暖正改变这一带的海洋生物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maritime</td><td>/ˈmerəˌtaɪm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>海事的，沿海的</td><td>maritime law<br>海事法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dispute was settled under international maritime law.  这场争端依国际海事法解决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 navigational</td><td>/ˌnævəˈɡeɪʃənəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>航行的，导航的</td><td>navigational equipment<br>导航设备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A navigational error sent the ferry far off course.  一次导航失误使渡轮严重偏航。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aeronautic</td><td>/ˌerəˈnɔːtɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>航空的，飞行的</td><td>aeronautic engineering<br>航空工程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He studied aeronautic engineering before joining the airline.  他在加入航空公司前学的是航空工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>naysay</b></del></td><td>/ˈneɪseɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拒绝，否认</td><td>naysay the proposal<br>否认这项提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Board members naysay every proposal that requires spending money on new equipment.  董事会成员否决每一项需要花钱买新设备的提议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decline</td><td>/dɪˈklaɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝；下降，衰退</td><td>decline an invitation<br>谢绝邀请</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She politely declined the invitation to speak at the ceremony.  她礼貌地谢绝了在典礼上发言的邀请。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deny</td><td>/dɪˈnaɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认；拒绝给予</td><td>flatly deny the charge<br>断然否认指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I do not deny that the plan carries some very real risks.  我不否认这个计划确实带有一些实实在在的风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 deject</del></td><td>/dɪˈdʒekt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使沮丧</td><td>deject the whole team<br>使全队沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing dejects a coach more than seeing his players quit trying.  没有什么比看到球员放弃努力更让教练沮丧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disallow</td><td>/ˌdɪsəˈlaʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不许，驳回</td><td>disallow the goal<br>判进球无效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The referee disallowed the goal for an obvious offside.  裁判因明显越位判该进球无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gainsay</td><td>/ˌɡeɪnˈseɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认</td><td>gainsay the evidence<br>否认证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could gainsay the evidence the inspector had collected.  没有人能否认检查员收集到的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oppose</td><td>/əˈpoʊz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>反对，抵制</td><td>oppose the new law<br>反对新法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two senators openly opposed the new tax bill.  两位参议员公开反对这项新税案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refuse</td><td>/rɪˈfjuːz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，不肯；废弃物</td><td>refuse to cooperate<br>拒绝合作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness refused to answer any further questions in court.  证人拒绝在法庭上回答任何进一步的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reject</td><td>/rɪˈdʒekt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>拒绝，驳回；废品</td><td>reject the proposal<br>否决提案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee voted to reject the proposal without further discussion.  委员会投票否决了该提案，不再讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accede</td><td>/əkˈsiːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>同意，答应；就任</td><td>accede to a request<br>答应请求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government finally acceded to the union's demands.  政府最终同意了工会的要求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agree</td><td>/əˈɡriː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>同意，赞同；一致</td><td>agree to the terms<br>同意这些条款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally agreed to the new safety rules.  双方最终同意了新的安全规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concur</td><td>/kənˈkɜːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>同意，一致；同时发生</td><td>concur with the judge<br>同意法官的意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two other judges concurred with the opinion she had written.  另外两位法官同意她撰写的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consent</td><td>/kənˈsent/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>同意，赞同</td><td>give one's consent<br>表示同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children cannot travel without their parents' consent.  没有父母的同意，孩子们不能出行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nebulous</b></td><td>/ˈnebjələs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的</td><td>a nebulous plan<br>模糊不清的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager presented a nebulous plan with no dates, budgets, or clear goals.  经理提出了一个没有日期、预算或明确目标的模糊计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambiguous</td><td>/æmˈbɪɡjuəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，含义不明的</td><td>an ambiguous reply<br>含糊其辞的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract's wording is ambiguous and invites future disputes.  合同措辞含义不明，容易引发日后争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arcane</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocal</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模棱两可的，含糊的</td><td>an equivocal answer<br>模棱两可的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness gave an equivocal account of that evening.  证人对那晚的叙述模棱两可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hazy</td><td>/ˈheɪzi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊的，朦胧的</td><td>a hazy recollection<br>模糊的记忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I have only a hazy recollection of that summer.  我对那个夏天只有模糊的记忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indistinct</td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>模糊不清的，含糊的</td><td>an indistinct outline<br>模糊的轮廓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph shows an indistinct figure by the door.  照片上门边有个模糊的人影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddy</td><td>/ˈmʌdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>浑浊的，不清晰的；使难以理解</td><td>muddy the argument<br>把论点搅浑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vague statistics only muddy the argument instead of settling it.  含糊的统计数据只会把论点搅浑，而不能解决争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 murky</td><td>/ˈmɜːrki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>昏暗的，阴暗的；晦涩不清的</td><td>a murky, windowless basement<br>昏暗无窗的地下室</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The murky basement was lit by one failing bulb.  昏暗的地下室里只有一只快坏的灯泡照明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obscure</td><td>/əbˈskjʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>含义模糊的；平凡的，不知名的</td><td>an obscure poet<br>不知名的诗人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an obscure poet until a critic rediscovered his work decades later.  他一直是个不知名的诗人，直到几十年后一位批评家重新发现他的作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 occult</td><td>/əˈkʌlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>occult knowledge<br>玄奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript is filled with occult knowledge that scholars still cannot decipher.  这份手稿满是学者至今无法解读的玄奥知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vague</td><td>/veɪɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>表达不清的</td><td>a vague answer<br>含糊的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His vague answer told the reporters almost nothing about the plan.  他含糊的回答几乎没有向记者透露任何计划内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distinct</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰的，明显的</td><td>a distinct impression<br>清晰的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a distinct difference between the two dialects.  这两种方言之间有明显差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>needy</b></td><td>/ˈniːdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；情感上过度依赖的</td><td>needy families<br>贫困家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charity distributes winter coats to needy children.  这家慈善机构向贫困儿童发放冬衣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 destitute</td><td>/ˈdestəˌtuːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的，穷困潦倒的</td><td>left destitute by war<br>因战争而穷困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands of refugees arrived destitute and without documents.  数千名难民抵达时穷困潦倒且没有证件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impecunious</td><td>/ˌɪmpɪˈkjuːniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫穷的，没钱的</td><td>an impecunious student<br>身无分文的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As an impecunious young painter, he lived on bread and tea.  作为一个穷困的年轻画家，他靠面包和茶为生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impoverished</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑːvərɪʃt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；贫瘠的</td><td>an impoverished region<br>贫困地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 War left the once-prosperous city utterly impoverished for many years.  战争让这座曾繁荣的城市多年极度贫困。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indigent</td><td>/ˈɪndɪdʒənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫穷的，贫困的</td><td>indigent defendants<br>贫困的被告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clinic provides free care to indigent patients.  这家诊所为贫困病人提供免费医疗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 penurious</del></td><td>/pəˈnʊriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫困的；吝啬的</td><td>penurious circumstances<br>贫困的境况</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent a penurious youth in a crowded tenement.  他在拥挤的廉租房里度过了贫困的青年时期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 poor</td><td>/pʊr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的，贫乏的；贫穷的</td><td>poor rocky soil<br>贫瘠的石质土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The harvest was poor after a whole summer without rain.  整个夏天没下雨，收成很差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threadbare</td><td>/ˈθredber/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；破旧的</td><td>a threadbare excuse<br>老掉牙的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered the same threadbare excuse for arriving late again.  他又用同一个老掉牙的借口解释迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 affluent</td><td>/ˈæfluənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富裕的，丰富的</td><td>an affluent suburb<br>富裕的郊区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved to a more affluent neighborhood after the promotion.  升职后他们搬到了更富裕的街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 opulent</td><td>/ˈɑːpjələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富裕的；华丽的</td><td>an opulent hotel lobby<br>豪华的酒店大厅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The opulent ballroom was lined with gold mirrors and velvet chairs.  这间华丽的舞厅摆满了金框镜子和天鹅绒椅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wealthy</td><td>/ˈwelθi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富有的，富裕的</td><td>a wealthy merchant<br>富有的商人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only the wealthy families could afford such an education.  只有富有的家庭才负担得起这样的教育。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nefarious</b></td><td>/nɪˈferiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，邪恶的</td><td>nefarious schemes<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators uncovered nefarious schemes to divert relief money into private accounts.  调查人员揭露了把救灾款转入私人账户的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 atrocious</td><td>/əˈtroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极坏的，极其残忍的</td><td>an atrocious crime<br>残暴的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the killing as an atrocious crime.  法庭称这起杀人事件是极其残忍的罪行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 degenerate</td><td>/dɪˈdʒenəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、不及物动词</td><td>衰退的，堕落的；恶化</td><td>degenerate into chaos<br>恶化为混乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaceful protest began to degenerate into shouting and shoving.  和平抗议开始恶化为叫骂和推挤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devious</td><td>/ˈdiːviəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡猾的</td><td>devious tactics<br>狡诈的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used devious means to obtain a copy of the secret contract.  他用狡猾的手段弄到了那份秘密合同的副本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evil</td><td>/ˈiːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>邪恶；邪恶的</td><td>the lesser evil<br>较小的祸害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel examines how ordinary people come to commit evil.  这部小说探讨普通人如何走向作恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heinous</td><td>/ˈheɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，十恶不赦的</td><td>a heinous act<br>令人憎恶的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court described the crime as heinous and utterly without excuse.  法庭称这一罪行十恶不赦，毫无借口可言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infamous</td><td>/ˈɪnfəməs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>臭名昭著的</td><td>an infamous crime<br>臭名昭著的罪行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dictator remains infamous for the brutal purges he ordered during his rule.  这位独裁者因其统治期间下令进行的残酷清洗而臭名昭著。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miscreant</td><td>/ˈmɪskriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶棍，罪犯；邪恶的</td><td>punish the miscreants<br>惩罚这些恶棍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge promised to punish the miscreants who had vandalized the village school.  法官承诺惩罚那些破坏村校的恶棍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vicious</td><td>/ˈvɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶恶的，恶毒的</td><td>a vicious circle<br>恶性循环</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dog gave a vicious bite that required several stitches.  那只狗凶恶地咬了一口，需要缝几针。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 villainous</td><td>/ˈvɪlənəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的，恶劣的</td><td>a villainous plot<br>邪恶的阴谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel turns on a villainous plot against the king.  这部小说围绕一个针对国王的邪恶阴谋展开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wicked</td><td>/ˈwɪkɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的</td><td>a wicked scheme<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wicked scheme to seize the estate was uncovered by the lawyer.  律师揭穿了一个夺取遗产的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ethical</td><td>/ˈeθɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>道德的，合乎道义的</td><td>ethical standards<br>道德标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters must weigh ethical limits before publishing private letters.  记者发表私人信件前必须权衡道德界限。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 righteous</td><td>/ˈraɪtʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的，正义的</td><td>righteous indignation<br>义愤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her righteous indignation at the ruling filled the courtroom.  她对判决的义愤充满法庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 upright</td><td>/ˈʌpraɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的，诚实的；竖直的</td><td>an upright citizen<br>一位正直的公民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors described him as an upright and reliable man.  邻居们说他是个正直可靠的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 virtuous</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>品德高尚的，正直的</td><td>a virtuous life<br>高尚的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a quiet, virtuous life and helped his neighbors freely.  他过着平静高尚的生活，乐于帮助邻里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>negation</b></td><td>/nɪˈɡeɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>否定</td><td>the negation of freedom<br>对自由的否定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Censorship of this kind amounts to the negation of freedom of thought.  这种审查等于是对思想自由的否定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contradiction</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈdɪkʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>矛盾，对立；反驳</td><td>a glaring contradiction<br>明显的矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a glaring contradiction between his speech and his voting record.  他的演讲与投票记录之间存在明显矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 denegation</del></td><td>/ˌdɛnɪˈɡeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>否认，拒绝</td><td>a flat denegation<br>断然否认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flat denegation of the charge convinced nobody.  他对指控的断然否认没说服任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denial</td><td>/dɪˈnaɪəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>否认，拒绝</td><td>issue a denial<br>发表否认声明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ministry issued a brief denial the same evening.  当晚该部就发表了简短的否认声明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 gainsaying</del></td><td>/ˌɡeɪnˈseɪɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>否认，反驳</td><td>there is no gainsaying it<br>这是无可否认的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is no gainsaying the value of her early research.  她早期研究的价值无可否认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rejection</td><td>/rɪˈdʒekʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>拒绝，否决</td><td>a flat rejection<br>断然拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee's rejection of the plan surprised no one.  委员会否决该方案并不令人意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repudiation</td><td>/rɪˌpjuːdiˈeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>否认，断绝关系</td><td>repudiation of the debt<br>拒付债务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government's repudiation of the debt shocked its creditors.  政府拒付债务震惊了债权人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acknowledgement</td><td>/əkˈnɑːlɪdʒmənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>承认；致谢</td><td>an acknowledgement of guilt<br>承认罪责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The letter contained a quiet acknowledgement of his mistake.  信中含着他对错误的低调承认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 affirmation</td><td>/ˌæfərˈmeɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>肯定，断言</td><td>a clear affirmation<br>明确的肯定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vote was a clear affirmation of the new charter.  这次投票是对新章程的明确肯定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 avowal</td><td>/əˈvaʊəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>承认，公开宣布</td><td>a public avowal<br>公开表明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His public avowal of support surprised everyone at the meeting.  他公开表明支持，令会上所有人意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>negligent</b></td><td>/ˈneɡlɪdʒənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疏忽大意的</td><td>negligent in his duties<br>对职责疏忽大意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company was found negligent in maintaining its equipment.  该公司被认定在设备维护上存在疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careless</td><td>/ˈkerləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，疏忽的</td><td>a careless mistake<br>粗心的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark ruined months of patient negotiation.  一句不慎的话毁了数月的耐心谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 derelict</td><td>/ˈderəlɪkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>玩忽职守的，不认真的</td><td>derelict in his duty<br>玩忽职守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard was found derelict in his duty on the night of the theft.  那名警卫被认定在失窃当夜玩忽职守。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heedless</td><td>/ˈhiːdləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不注意的，不顾的</td><td>heedless of danger<br>不顾危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ran into the street heedless of the traffic.  他不顾车流冲进了街道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neglectful</td><td>/nɪˈɡlektfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疏忽的，不留心的</td><td>neglectful of his duties<br>疏忽职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report found the agency neglectful of basic safety inspections.  报告认定该机构疏忽了基本安全检查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remiss</td><td>/rɪˈmɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疏忽的，不留心的</td><td>be remiss in one's duties<br>疏于职守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I would be remiss if I did not thank the volunteers.  如果我不感谢志愿者，那就是我的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attentive</td><td>/əˈtentɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，专注的；周到的</td><td>an attentive audience<br>专注的听众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse stayed attentive to every change in his breathing.  护士留心他呼吸的每一点变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cautious</td><td>/ˈkɔːʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>a cautious approach<br>谨慎的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors remained cautious until the election results were announced.  在选举结果公布前投资者一直很谨慎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 heedful</del></td><td>/ˈhiːdfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，注意的</td><td>heedful of the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heedful of the forecast, the climbers turned back early.  留意到天气预报，登山者提早返回了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>negotiate</b></td><td>/nɪˈɡoʊʃieɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>商量，谈判</td><td>negotiate a contract<br>谈判一份合同</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides negotiated a contract that satisfied neither of them completely.  双方谈成了一份合同，但双方都不完全满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrange</td><td>/əˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>安排，筹划；整理</td><td>arrange the meeting<br>安排会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She arranged the whole trip in a single busy afternoon.  她在一个忙碌的下午就安排好了整趟行程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bargain</td><td>/ˈbɑːrɡən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>协议；讨价还价</td><td>drive a hard bargain<br>狠狠地讨价还价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dealer likes to drive a hard bargain with new customers.  这个商人喜欢和新客户狠狠地讨价还价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 concert</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>协力，一致；音乐会</td><td>act in concert<br>协同行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The agencies acted in concert to trace the missing funds.  各机构协同行动追查失踪的资金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 settle</td><td>/ˈsetəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>解决，平息；定居</td><td>settle the dispute<br>解决争端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two sides finally settled their dispute out of court.  双方最终在庭外解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 break down</td><td>/breɪk ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>分解；故障，崩溃</td><td>break down into parts<br>分解成若干部分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Bacteria break down the waste into simpler compounds.  细菌把废物分解成更简单的化合物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>neophyte</b></td><td>/ˈniːəfaɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>初学者，新手</td><td>a neophyte in politics<br>政界的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a neophyte in politics, he underestimated how slowly legislation actually moves.  作为政界的新手，他低估了立法进程实际有多缓慢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abecedarian</del></td><td>/ˌeɪbisiˈdɛriən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>初学者；初级的</td><td>an abecedarian student<br>初学的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even an abecedarian can follow these first three lessons.  连初学者都能跟上这最初三课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apprentice</td><td>/əˈprentəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>学徒，新手</td><td>a carpenter's apprentice<br>木匠学徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He started as an apprentice and now owns the shop.  他从学徒做起，现在拥有这家店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fledgling</td><td>/ˈfledʒlɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>新手</td><td>a fledgling company<br>新兴的公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fledgling airline struggled through its very first winter.  这家新兴航空公司艰难度过了第一个冬天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 freshman</td><td>/ˈfreʃmən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大一新生；新手</td><td>a freshman senator<br>新任参议员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a freshman he knew almost nobody on campus.  作为大一新生，他在校园里几乎谁也不认识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 novice</td><td>/ˈnɑːvɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，初学者</td><td>a complete novice<br>彻底的新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A complete novice, he had never held a chisel before that afternoon.  他是个彻底的新手，那天下午之前从没握过凿子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recruit</td><td>/rəˈkruːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>新成员，新兵；招募</td><td>a raw recruit<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every new recruit spends six weeks in basic training.  每名新兵都要接受六周基础训练。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rookie</td><td>/ˈrʊki/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>新兵；新手；菜鸟</td><td>a rookie mistake<br>新手才犯的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rookie made a nervous throw that cost his team the game.  那名新手一次紧张的传球让球队输掉了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tyro</td><td>/ˈtaɪroʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>新手，业余爱好者</td><td>a tyro at chess<br>国际象棋新手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a tyro at chess, he lost every game that afternoon.  作为国际象棋新手，他那天下午每局都输了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 veteran</td><td>/ˈvetərən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>老兵；有丰富经验的人</td><td>a veteran journalist<br>资深记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A veteran journalist, she has covered four wars and six elections.  作为资深记者，她报道过四场战争和六次选举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nerve</b></td><td>/nɜːrv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>（坚强的）意志，勇气</td><td>lose one's nerve<br>失去勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He lost his nerve at the last moment and never asked the question.  他在最后一刻失去了勇气，始终没有问出那个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bravery</td><td>/ˈbreɪvəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，英勇</td><td>an act of bravery<br>勇敢之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was given a medal for bravery during the flood.  她因在洪水中的勇敢而获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fortitude</td><td>/ˈfɔːrtɪˌtuːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>坚韧，刚毅</td><td>bear pain with fortitude<br>坚韧地忍受痛苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bore months of treatment with quiet fortitude.  她默默坚韧地承受了数月的治疗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guts</td><td>/ɡʌts/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，胆量；内脏</td><td>have the guts<br>有胆量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had the guts to quit and start her own company.  她有胆量辞职创办自己的公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrepidity</td><td>/ˌɪntrəˈpɪdəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，无畏</td><td>show great intrepidity<br>表现出极大勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot's intrepidity saved everyone aboard that night.  那晚飞行员的无畏救了机上所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resolution</td><td>/ˌrezəˈluːʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>决心，坚定；决议</td><td>act with resolution<br>果断坚定地行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced that difficult year with quiet resolution.  她以沉静的决心面对那艰难的一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stamina</td><td>/ˈstæmɪnə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>耐力</td><td>build up stamina<br>增强耐力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long morning swims helped him build up remarkable stamina for racing.  长时间的晨泳帮他练出比赛所需的惊人耐力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animate</td><td>/ˈænəmeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使有生气，激励；有活力的</td><td>animate the discussion<br>让讨论活跃起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few well-chosen questions can animate an otherwise dull discussion.  几个精心挑选的问题就能让本来沉闷的讨论活跃起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brace</td><td>/ˈbreɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使振作，鼓励；支撑</td><td>brace yourself for impact<br>做好承受冲击准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A cup of hot tea braced him for the long night.  一杯热茶让他为漫长的夜晚振作起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cheer</td><td>/ˈtʃɪr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>欢呼，喝彩；使高兴</td><td>a loud cheer<br>一阵响亮的欢呼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A loud cheer rose from the crowd when the goal was scored.  进球时人群中响起一阵响亮的欢呼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embolden</td><td>/ɪmˈboʊldən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，使大胆</td><td>embolden the rebels<br>壮了叛乱者的胆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Early success emboldened her to ask for a promotion.  初期的成功让她有胆量要求升职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 inspirit</del></td><td>/ɪnˈspɪrɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓舞，激励</td><td>inspirit the weary troops<br>鼓舞疲惫的部队</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her speech inspirited the exhausted volunteers to keep working through the night.  她的演讲鼓舞疲惫的志愿者继续通宵工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 steel</td><td>/ˈstiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使坚强，使下决心</td><td>steel oneself for bad news<br>为坏消息鼓起勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She steeled herself and walked into the interview room.  她鼓起勇气走进面试室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strengthen</td><td>/ˈstreŋθən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>加强，增强</td><td>strengthen the economy<br>增强经济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular exercise will strengthen your heart and lungs.  规律锻炼会增强你的心肺功能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cowardice</td><td>/ˈkaʊərdɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>怯懦，胆小</td><td>an act of cowardice<br>怯懦之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refusing to speak up for a friend struck him later as cowardice.  没有为朋友说话，事后他觉得那是怯懦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 pusillanimity</del></td><td>/ˌpjuːsələˈnɪməti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胆小，懦弱无志气</td><td>diplomatic pusillanimity<br>外交上的软弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their pusillanimity allowed the aggressor to seize the border towns unopposed.  他们的懦弱使侵略者不受阻碍地占领了边境城镇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nettle</b></td><td>/ˈnetl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>惹怒</td><td>nettled by criticism<br>被批评惹怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was clearly nettled by the reporter's rude question.  记者无礼的提问显然惹恼了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoy</td><td>/əˈnɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，不断烦扰</td><td>annoy the neighbors<br>不断打扰邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The barking dog began to annoy every neighbor on the quiet street.  那只不停犬吠的狗开始烦扰这条安静街上的每位邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exasperate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæspəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，激怒</td><td>exasperate his parents<br>使他的父母恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His constant lateness exasperated even the most patient teachers.  他总是迟到，连最有耐心的老师都被激怒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infuriate</td><td>/ɪnˈfjʊrieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>infuriate the critics<br>激怒批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision to cut pensions infuriated thousands of workers across the country.  削减养老金的决定激怒了全国成千上万的工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irritate</td><td>/ˈɪrɪteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒；刺激</td><td>irritate the customers<br>使顾客恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud advertisements before every video irritate most of our customers.  每个视频前的大声广告让我们大多数客户感到恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peeve</td><td>/piːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，惹恼</td><td>peeve the audience<br>惹恼观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud phone conversations on the train really peeve the other passengers.  火车上大声打电话确实惹恼其他乘客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pique</td><td>/piːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起（兴趣）；使生气</td><td>pique her curiosity<br>激起她的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange title piqued her curiosity, so she borrowed the book.  奇怪的书名激起了她的好奇心，于是她借走了那本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 provoke</td><td>/prəˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒；驱使，激起</td><td>provoke an angry response<br>引发愤怒的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark provoked an angry response from the whole audience.  他不慎的言论激起了全体观众的愤怒回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roil</td><td>/rɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>roil the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The careless remarks of the mayor roiled the crowd outside the courthouse.  市长轻率的言论激怒了法院外的人群。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ruffle</td><td>/ˈrʌfl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>弄乱（毛发）；扰乱，使恼怒</td><td>ruffle his feathers<br>惹他不快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single rude question was enough to ruffle the normally calm witness.  一个无礼的问题就足以扰乱这位平素冷静的证人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conciliate</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；调解</td><td>conciliate angry customers<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager tried to conciliate the angry customers with refunds.  经理试图用退款来安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 propitiate</td><td>/prəˈpɪʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抚慰，劝解</td><td>propitiate the angry gods<br>抚慰愤怒的神灵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers offered rice and flowers to propitiate the mountain spirits.  村民献上米和花来抚慰山神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>neutralize</b></td><td>/ˈnuːtrəlaɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>中和；抵消，使无效</td><td>neutralize the acid<br>中和酸性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A spoonful of baking soda will neutralize the acid spilled on the bench.  一勺小苏打就能中和溅在台面上的酸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annul</td><td>/əˈnʌl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>宣告无效，取消</td><td>annul the marriage<br>宣告婚姻无效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court agreed to annul the marriage after only three weeks.  法院同意在仅仅三周之后宣告这段婚姻无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cancel</td><td>/ˈkænsəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>取消，撤销；抵消</td><td>cancel the meeting<br>取消会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They canceled the outdoor concert because of the heavy rain.  因为大雨，他们取消了露天音乐会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 counteract</td><td>/ˌkaʊntərˈækt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>抵抗，抵消，中和</td><td>counteract the poison<br>中和毒素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Exercise can counteract some effects of a poor diet.  运动可以抵消不良饮食的部分影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frustrate</td><td>/ˈfrʌstreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻挠，使受挫；使沮丧</td><td>frustrate one's plans<br>使计划受挫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow frustrated our plans to drive home.  大雪打乱了我们开车回家的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 negate</td><td>/nɪˈɡeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认；否定，取消</td><td>negate the effect<br>抵消效果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless error can negate weeks of careful work.  一个粗心的错误能抹掉数周的细致工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nullify</td><td>/ˈnʌləfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使无效，废除</td><td>nullify an agreement<br>使协议失效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge nullified the election result.  法官宣布选举结果无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 activate</td><td>/ˈæktəˌveɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激活，启动</td><td>activate the alarm<br>启动警报</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Smoke from the toaster activated the fire alarm.  烤面包机的烟启动了火警。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vitalize</td><td>/ˈvaɪtəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赋予活力，激励</td><td>vitalize the local economy<br>使当地经济充满活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New investment vitalized the sleepy town within just two years.  新投资在两年内使这个沉寂的小镇焕发活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nexus</b></td><td>/ˈneksəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>连结；核心，最重要的地带</td><td>the nexus of trade<br>贸易的枢纽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The port city became the nexus of trade between three neighboring empires.  这座港口城市成为三个邻近帝国之间贸易的枢纽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bond</td><td>/ˈbɑːnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使结合，联结</td><td>bond the layers together<br>把各层黏合起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The glue bonds metal to glass in seconds.  这种胶数秒内就能把金属与玻璃黏合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 connection</td><td>/kəˈnekʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>联系，连结</td><td>a close connection<br>密切的联系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a close connection between sleep and memory.  研究者发现睡眠与记忆有密切联系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 link</td><td>/ˈlɪŋk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>联系，纽带；连接</td><td>a close link<br>紧密的联系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a close link between sleep and memory.  研究者发现睡眠与记忆之间存在紧密联系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tie</td><td>/ˈtaɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>纽带，关系；系，绑</td><td>family ties<br>家庭纽带</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Strong family ties kept the brothers in the same town.  牢固的家庭纽带让兄弟俩留在同一个镇上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 base</td><td>/ˈbeɪs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣的，低下的</td><td>base motives<br>卑劣的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He acted from base motives of greed and envy.  他出于贪婪和嫉妒的卑劣动机行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 capital</td><td>/ˈkæpətəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>中心地，中心；首都</td><td>the fashion capital<br>时尚之都</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Milan is often called the fashion capital of Italy.  米兰常被称为意大利的时尚之都。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 center</td><td>/ˈsentər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使集中；中心</td><td>center attention on quality<br>把注意力集中于质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report centers its argument on three very clear examples.  报告把论点集中在三个非常清晰的例子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 core</td><td>/ˈkɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点</td><td>the core of the argument<br>论点的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At the core of the dispute lies a simple money question.  争端的核心是一个简单的金钱问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 focus</td><td>/ˈfoʊkəs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>聚集，集中；焦点</td><td>focus attention on<br>把注意力集中于</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lens focuses sunlight into a single bright point.  透镜把阳光聚集成一个明亮的点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kernel</td><td>/ˈkɜːrnəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点；果核</td><td>the kernel of truth<br>一点真实的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a kernel of truth in this otherwise absurd rumor.  这个荒唐的谣言里有一点真实的核心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heart</td><td>/ˈhɑːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，中心；心脏</td><td>the heart of the matter<br>问题的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Let us get to the heart of the matter quickly.  我们快点谈到问题的核心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hub</td><td>/hʌb/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>中心，枢纽</td><td>a transport hub<br>交通枢纽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old port grew into a busy transport hub.  这座旧港发展成繁忙的交通枢纽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nucleus</td><td>/ˈnuːkliəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，中心；原子核</td><td>the nucleus of the team<br>队伍的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Four veterans formed the nucleus of the new team.  四名老将构成了新队的核心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 margin</td><td>/ˈmɑːrdʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>边缘；页边空白；差额</td><td>the margin of the lake<br>湖的边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tall reeds grow along the margin of the lake.  高高的芦苇沿着湖边生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 periphery</td><td>/pəˈrɪfəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>外围，边缘</td><td>the periphery of the city<br>城市的外围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New factories sprang up on the periphery of the city.  新工厂在城市外围拔地而起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nibble</b></td><td>/ˈnɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>小口咬</td><td>nibble at the cheese<br>小口啃干酪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mouse nibbled at the cheese we had left beside the kitchen door.  老鼠小口啃着我们放在厨房门边的干酪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bite</td><td>/ˈbaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>咬；一口</td><td>take a bite<br>咬一口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took only one small bite and pushed the plate away.  她只咬了一小口就把盘子推开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nip</td><td>/nɪp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小口吃喝</td><td>a nip of whisky<br>一小口威士忌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He poured himself a nip of whisky and sat down by the fire.  他给自己倒了一小口威士忌，坐到火边。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peck</td><td>/pek/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>小口啄食，小口地吃</td><td>peck at the food<br>小口啄食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nervous child only pecked at her dinner and left the table early.  紧张的孩子只小口吃了几下晚饭就早早离席。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sip</td><td>/ˈsɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>小口喝，抿</td><td>sip hot tea slowly<br>慢慢抿热茶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sipped her tea and watched the rain through the window.  她抿着茶，透过窗子看雨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tipple</td><td>/ˈtɪpəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>常喝酒，小酌；酒</td><td>tipple in the evening<br>傍晚小酌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sailor tippled quietly by the fire every evening.  这位老水手每晚都在火炉边静静小酌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gobble</td><td>/ˈɡɑːbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>狼吞虎咽</td><td>gobble down the meal<br>狼吞虎咽吃完饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children gobbled down their sandwiches and ran outside to play again.  孩子们狼吞虎咽吃完三明治，又跑到外面去玩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nicety</b></td><td>/ˈnaɪsəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>准确，精确</td><td>with great nicety<br>极为精确地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The instruments measure temperature with great nicety, down to a hundredth of a degree.  这些仪器极为精确地测量温度，精确到百分之一度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accuracy</td><td>/ˈækjərəsi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>准确性，精确度</td><td>a high degree of accuracy<br>很高的精确度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new sensor measures temperature with surprising accuracy.  新传感器测温精确度出人意料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicacy</td><td>/ˈdelɪkəsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（外貌、结构等）精致</td><td>the delicacy of the lace<br>蕾丝的精致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone admired the delicacy of the carving on the ivory box.  大家都赞叹象牙盒上雕刻的精致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exactness</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæktnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精确，严谨</td><td>mathematical exactness<br>数学般的精确</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He described the scene with almost photographic exactness.  他几乎以照片般的精确描述了那一幕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fineness</td><td>/ˈfaɪnnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>精细，细微</td><td>the fineness of the thread<br>线的精细程度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fineness of the carving amazed every visitor.  雕刻的精细令每位参观者惊叹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precision</td><td>/priˈsɪʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精确，精密</td><td>with military precision<br>以军事般的精确</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team executed the plan with military precision.  团队以军事般的精确执行了计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 veracity</td><td>/vəˈræsəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>真实性，诚实</td><td>question his veracity<br>质疑他的真实性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody seriously questioned the veracity of her account.  没人真正质疑她叙述的真实性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 coarseness</td><td>/ˈkɔːrsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>粗糙；粗俗</td><td>the coarseness of the fabric<br>布料的粗糙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coarseness of the cheap fabric irritated her sensitive skin.  廉价布料的粗糙刺激了她敏感的皮肤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 imprecision</td><td>/ˌɪmprɪˈsɪʒən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不精确，含糊</td><td>the imprecision of the data<br>数据的不精确</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Imprecision in the early measurements ruined the whole study.  早期测量的不精确毁了整项研究。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inaccuracy</td><td>/ˌɪnˈækjərəsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不准确，错误</td><td>factual inaccuracy<br>事实上的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers pointed out several inaccuracies in the article.  读者指出文章中几处不准确之处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 roughness</td><td>/ˈrʌfnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>粗糙；粗暴，粗鲁</td><td>the roughness of his manner<br>他举止的粗鲁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The roughness of his reply startled everyone at the table.  他回答的粗鲁让满桌人都吃了一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nil</b></td><td>/nɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>不存在，零</td><td>reduced to nil<br>减至零</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our chances of finishing before dark were reduced to nil by the storm.  暴风雨把我们天黑前完工的机会降到了零。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nothing</td><td>/ˈnʌθɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>代词、名词</td><td>无，没有东西</td><td>come to nothing<br>毫无结果</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once a political nonentity, he suddenly became the party's most quoted voice.  他曾是政坛的无名之辈，突然成了党内被引用最多的声音。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td>↳反 existence</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɪstəns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>存在，生存</td><td>come into existence<br>开始存在</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 bit</td><td>/ˈbɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>一点，少量</td><td>a bit of luck<br>一点运气</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sipped her tea and watched the rain through the window.  她抿着茶，透过窗子看雨。</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 tipple</td><td>/ˈtɪpəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>常喝酒，小酌；酒</td><td>tipple in the evening<br>傍晚小酌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sailor tippled quietly by the fire every evening.  这位老水手每晚都在火炉边静静小酌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abundance</td><td>/əˈbʌndəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>丰富，大量</td><td>an abundance of fresh fruit<br>大量新鲜水果</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳反 affluence</td><td>/ˈæfluəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>富裕，丰富</td><td>live in affluence<br>生活富裕</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 avalanche</td><td>/ˈævəˌlæntʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>雪崩；大量涌来</td><td>an avalanche of letters<br>如雪崩般的信件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The announcement triggered an avalanche of angry emails.  这则公告引发了如雪崩般的愤怒邮件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guzzle</td><td>/ˈɡʌzl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>狂饮</td><td>guzzle a can of beer<br>狂饮一罐啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He guzzled two bottles of soda before the game even started.  比赛还没开始他就狂饮了两瓶汽水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quaff</td><td>/kwæf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛饮；大口喝</td><td>quaff a mug of ale<br>大口喝下一杯麦酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thirsty travelers quaffed mugs of ale at the roadside inn.  口渴的旅人在路边客栈痛饮大杯麦酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 swill</td><td>/ˈswɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>痛饮，大口喝；冲洗</td><td>swill down a beer<br>痛饮一杯啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They swilled cheap beer all night in the noisy bar.  他们在嘈杂的酒吧里痛饮廉价啤酒一整夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nitpick</b></td><td>/ˈnɪtpɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>吹毛求疵</td><td>nitpick about grammar<br>在语法上吹毛求疵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers nitpick about grammar while ignoring the serious flaws in the argument.  评审者在语法上吹毛求疵，却忽视论证中的严重缺陷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carp</td><td>/kɑːrp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>对小事吹毛求疵，挑剔，表现不满</td><td>carp at every detail<br>对每个细节都挑剔</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cavil</td><td>/ˈkævl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>挑剔，吹毛求疵</td><td>cavil over wording<br>在措辞上吹毛求疵</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fuss</td><td>/fʌs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小题大做，抱怨；忙乱</td><td>make a fuss about<br>为…大惊小怪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't make a fuss about a tiny stain on the tablecloth.  别为桌布上一小块污渍小题大做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 niggle</td><td>/ˈnɪɡəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>对琐事挑剔；使烦扰</td><td>niggle over trifles<br>为琐事吹毛求疵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers niggled over commas instead of judging the argument.  评审者在逗号上挑剔，却不评判论点本身。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quibble</td><td>/ˈkwɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>吹毛求疵</td><td>quibble over details<br>在细节上挑刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They quibbled over the wording while the deadline slipped away.  他们在措辞上纠缠不休，截止日期却悄悄过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nocturnal</b></td><td>/nɑːkˈtɜːrnl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>夜间的</td><td>nocturnal hunters<br>夜行猎手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Owls and other nocturnal hunters rely on hearing far more than on sight.  猫头鹰等夜行猎手对听觉的依赖远超视觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nightly</td><td>/ˈnaɪtli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>每夜的，夜间的</td><td>a nightly ritual<br>每晚的例行事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reading aloud became their nightly ritual before bed.  朗读成了他们睡前每晚的例行事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nighttime</td><td>/ˈnaɪtˌtaɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>夜间的；夜间</td><td>nighttime temperatures<br>夜间气温</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nighttime temperatures fell well below freezing all week.  整周夜间气温都降到冰点以下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 daily</td><td>/ˈdeɪli/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>每日的，日常的</td><td>a daily routine<br>日常作息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short walk is part of his daily routine.  短距离散步是他日常作息的一部分。</td></tr>
