<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most birds are diurnal, hunting and singing during daylight hours.  大多数鸟类是昼行的，在白天觅食和鸣叫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>noisome</b></td><td>/ˈnɔɪsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的；令人极厌恶的</td><td>a noisome odor<br>一股恶臭气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers refused to enter the noisome tunnel without masks.  工人们不戴口罩不肯进入那条恶臭的隧道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fetid</td><td>/ˈfetɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的</td><td>fetid air<br>恶臭的空气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fetid smell rose from the blocked drain behind the house.  屋后堵塞的下水道散出恶臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foul</td><td>/ˈfaʊl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>玷污（名誉）；弄脏</td><td>foul one's good name<br>玷污自己的好名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal fouled the good name he had spent decades building.  这桩丑闻玷污了他数十年积累的好名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malodorous</td><td>/ˌmælˈoʊdərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的</td><td>malodorous waste<br>恶臭的废弃物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors traced the malodorous discharge to a nearby factory.  检查人员追查到那股恶臭排放来自附近一家工厂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smelly</td><td>/ˈsmeli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难闻的，有臭味的</td><td>smelly socks<br>有臭味的袜子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bin in the corner was getting distinctly smelly.  角落里的垃圾桶明显开始发臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stinky</td><td>/ˈstɪŋki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>臭的，恶臭的</td><td>a stinky cheese<br>一种臭味的奶酪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That stinky cheese tastes far better than it smells.  那种臭奶酪的味道远比气味好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abhorrent</td><td>/æbˈhɔːrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>abhorrent to most people<br>令多数人厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The practice is abhorrent to anyone with basic decency.  这种做法令任何有基本良知的人厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appalling</td><td>/əˈpɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>骇人的，糟糕透顶的</td><td>appalling living conditions<br>骇人的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers lived in appalling conditions close to the mine.  工人们住在矿区附近骇人的条件中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disgusting</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a disgusting smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was left in a disgusting state after the party.  聚会后厨房脏得让人恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loathsome</td><td>/ˈloʊðsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>a loathsome smell<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A loathsome smell rose from the neglected drain.  无人清理的下水道升起令人厌恶的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nauseating</td><td>/ˈnɔːziˌeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人恶心的</td><td>a nauseating stench<br>令人恶心的臭味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nauseating stench drove everyone out of the kitchen.  令人恶心的臭味把所有人赶出厨房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 offensive</td><td>/əˈfensɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人反感的；无礼的</td><td>an offensive remark<br>无礼的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His offensive remark cost him the audience's goodwill.  他无礼的言论让他失去观众好感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repugnant</td><td>/rɪˈpʌɡnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>morally repugnant behavior<br>道德上可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most readers found the cruelty of that character morally repugnant and unforgivable.  多数读者觉得那个角色的残忍在道德上令人厌恶，不可宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repulsive</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a repulsive sight<br>令人恶心的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rotting fish on the beach was absolutely repulsive.  沙滩上腐烂的鱼令人极其恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ambrosial</td><td>/æmˈbroʊziəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特别美味的；芬芳的</td><td>an ambrosial dessert<br>美味无比的甜点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup had an ambrosial aroma that filled the whole house.  这汤散发的美妙香气弥漫了整座房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aromatic</td><td>/ˌerəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的</td><td>aromatic herbs<br>芳香的草药</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was full of aromatic spices from the market.  厨房里满是从市场买来的芳香香料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fragrant</td><td>/ˈfreɪɡrənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的，香的</td><td>fragrant flowers<br>芳香的花朵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was fragrant with fresh bread and cinnamon.  厨房里弥漫着新鲜面包和肉桂的香气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perfumed</td><td>/pərˈfjuːmd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有香味的，洒了香水的</td><td>perfumed soap<br>香皂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote the letter on thin perfumed paper.  她用薄薄的香纸写了那封信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 redolent</td><td>/ˈredələnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的；令人想起…的</td><td>redolent of spices<br>散发香料气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old room was redolent of pipe smoke and leather.  那间旧屋弥漫着烟斗烟味和皮革气息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 savory</td><td>/ˈseɪvəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a savory dish<br>一道可口的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a savory pie made with mushrooms and herbs.  她端上一道用蘑菇和香草做的美味派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scented</td><td>/ˈsentɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有香味的，加香的</td><td>scented candles<br>香味蜡烛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote all her letters on pale scented paper.  她所有的信都写在淡色的香味纸上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sweet</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>甜的；温和的，亲切的</td><td>in a sweet tone<br>以温和的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a sweet voice that calmed the frightened child.  她用温柔的声音说话，安抚了害怕的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appealing</td><td>/əˈpiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的</td><td>an appealing offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made an appealing offer that she could hardly refuse.  公司提出了一个她几乎无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 captivating</td><td>/ˈkæptɪˌveɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，令人着迷的</td><td>a captivating performance<br>令人着迷的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her captivating voice held the entire audience in complete silence.  她迷人的歌声让全场观众鸦雀无声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enchanting</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，醉人的</td><td>an enchanting melody<br>醉人的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden looked enchanting under the soft light of lanterns.  花园在灯笼柔和的光线下显得十分迷人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fascinating</td><td>/ˈfæsəˌneɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，极有趣的</td><td>a fascinating discovery<br>引人入胜的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Insect behavior is a fascinating subject for young scientists.  昆虫行为对年轻科学家来说是极有趣的课题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nomad</b></td><td>/ˈnoʊmæd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>游牧者，居无定所的人</td><td>a desert nomad<br>沙漠中的游牧者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A desert nomad guided us across the dunes toward the hidden well.  一位沙漠游牧者带我们穿过沙丘走向隐蔽的水井。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambulant</td><td>/ˈæmbjələnt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>流动的；能走动的</td><td>an ambulant patient<br>能走动的病人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clinic treats ambulant patients who do not need a bed.  这家诊所治疗无需住院的能走动病人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fugitive</td><td>/ˈfjuːdʒətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>短暂的，易逝的；逃亡的</td><td>a fugitive impression<br>转瞬即逝的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fugitive smile crossed her face before she resumed her serious expression.  一丝转瞬即逝的笑意掠过她的脸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gallivanting</td><td>/ˈɡælɪvæntɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>闲游的，四处游荡的</td><td>gallivanting about town<br>在城里四处闲逛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent the summer gallivanting about the southern coast.  他整个夏天在南部海岸四处闲游。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 perambulatory</del></td><td>/pəˈræmbjələtɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫游的，巡行的</td><td>a perambulatory habit<br>漫游的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old scholar kept his perambulatory habit into old age.  这位老学者到晚年仍保持漫游的习惯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peripatetic</td><td>/ˌperipəˈtetɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巡游的，四处游历的</td><td>a peripatetic career<br>四处奔波的职业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peripatetic photographer rarely slept in the same city twice.  这位四处游历的摄影师很少在同一座城市住两次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ranging</td><td>/ˈreɪndʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>游走的，漫游的</td><td>far-ranging herds<br>远距游走的畜群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wide-ranging herds cross the plateau twice each year.  游走范围极广的畜群每年两次穿越高原。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roaming</td><td>/ˈroʊmɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫游的，四处游荡的</td><td>roaming herds of cattle<br>四处游荡的牛群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Roaming bands of musicians played in every square that summer.  那年夏天，漫游的乐队在每个广场演奏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vagabond</td><td>/ˈvæɡəbɑnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>流浪者；流浪的</td><td>a vagabond life<br>流浪的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a vagabond life among the river towns.  他在河边小镇间过着流浪生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vagrant</td><td>/ˈveɪɡrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>流浪者；漂泊的</td><td>a vagrant existence<br>漂泊的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Charity workers found the vagrant sleeping under the bridge.  慈善工作者发现那名流浪者睡在桥下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wayfaring</td><td>/ˈweɪfɛrɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>徒步旅行的，漂泊的</td><td>a wayfaring stranger<br>漂泊的旅人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wayfaring stranger asked for shelter at the farmhouse.  一位漂泊的旅人到农舍求宿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 settled</td><td>/ˈsetəld/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>固定的，安定的</td><td>a settled routine<br>固定的作息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years of moving they finally led a settled life.  多年漂泊后他们终于过上安定的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nominal</b></td><td>/ˈnɑːmɪnl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>名义上的；微不足道的</td><td>a nominal fee<br>象征性的费用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum charges a nominal fee that barely covers the cost of printing tickets.  博物馆收取象征性的费用，几乎只够印门票的成本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inconsiderable</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微不足道的</td><td>an inconsiderable sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk is small but by no means inconsiderable.  风险虽小，却绝非微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paltry</td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 big</td><td>/ˈbɪɡ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，大幅的；重要的</td><td>a big salary increase<br>大幅加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was offered a big raise and took the job at once.  她获得大幅加薪，立刻接受了这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considerable</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（数量上）可观（而值得注意）的</td><td>a considerable sum<br>一笔可观的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The renovation required a considerable amount of money and nearly two years of work.  这次翻修耗费了可观的资金和近两年的工期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 important</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的</td><td>an important decision<br>一个重要的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sleep is as important as diet for a young athlete.  对年轻运动员来说，睡眠和饮食一样重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 material</td><td>/məˈtɪriəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>物质的；重要的；材料</td><td>material comforts<br>物质上的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cared little for material wealth and lived very simply.  他不太在意物质财富，生活极为简朴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nonchalant</b></td><td>/ˌnɑːnʃəˈlɑːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫不经心的，淡然的</td><td>a nonchalant shrug<br>漫不经心地一耸肩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the accusation with a nonchalant shrug and changed the subject.  他冷淡地一耸肩回应指控，然后换了话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apathetic</td><td>/ˌæpəˈθetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠的，无兴趣的</td><td>apathetic voters<br>冷漠的选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most students seemed apathetic about the election and did not vote.  大多数学生对这次选举漠不关心，也没有投票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disinterested</td><td>/dɪsˈɪntrəstɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>公正的，无偏见的</td><td>a disinterested judgment<br>公正的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need a disinterested judgment from someone with no stake in the outcome.  我们需要一个与结果无涉的人作出公正的判断。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insensible</td><td>/ɪnˈsensəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无知觉的，昏迷的</td><td>insensible to pain<br>感觉不到疼痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blow left him insensible for several minutes.  这一击使他几分钟内失去了知觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insouciant</td><td>/ɪnˈsuːsiənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无忧虑的，不在乎的</td><td>an insouciant shrug<br>满不在乎地耸肩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the harsh criticism with an insouciant shrug and a smile.  他对严厉的批评只是满不在乎地耸耸肩，笑了笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perfunctory</td><td>/pərˈfʌŋktəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>敷衍的，草率的</td><td>a perfunctory nod<br>敷衍的点头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave the report a perfunctory glance and signed it immediately.  他敷衍地扫了一眼报告就立刻签了字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unconcerned</td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈsɜːrnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不关心的，漠不关心的</td><td>unconcerned about risk<br>对风险漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seemed entirely unconcerned about the noise from the street.  她似乎完全不在意街上的噪音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concerned</td><td>/kənˈsɜːrnd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>关心的，担忧的</td><td>concerned about the outcome<br>关心结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parents are deeply concerned about the new school policy.  家长们非常关心新的学校政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 interested</td><td>/ˈɪntrəstɪd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>感兴趣的</td><td>interested in the topic<br>对这个话题感兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became interested in astronomy at the age of nine.  她九岁时开始对天文学产生兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nondescript</b></td><td>/ˈnɑːndɪskrɪpt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平凡的，不吸引人的</td><td>a nondescript building<br>毫不起眼的楼房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They met in a nondescript building with no sign above the door.  他们在一座门上没有招牌、毫不起眼的楼房里见面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beige</td><td>/beɪʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏特征的</td><td>a beige personality<br>毫无特色的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report describes him as a beige figure with no strong views.  报告把他描述成一个没有鲜明主张、缺乏特征的人物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 characterless</td><td>/ˈkɛrəktərləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无特色的；缺乏骨气的</td><td>a characterless building<br>毫无特色的建筑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They moved into a clean but characterless apartment block.  他们搬进一栋干净却毫无特色的公寓楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 featureless</td><td>/ˈfiːtʃərləs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏特征的</td><td>a featureless plain<br>毫无特征的平原</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road crossed a flat, featureless plain for eighty miles.  这条路穿过八十英里平坦、毫无特征的平原。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 indistinctive</del></td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈstɪŋktɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无特色的，难以分辨的</td><td>an indistinctive style<br>毫无特色的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her indistinctive style made the paintings hard to date.  她毫无特色的风格使这些画难以断代。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vanilla</td><td>/vəˈnɪlə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平淡无奇的；香草</td><td>plain vanilla software<br>最普通的软件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a plain vanilla version without extra features.  他们选了没有额外功能的最普通版本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conspicuous</td><td>/kənˈspɪkjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>显而易见的；吸引人的</td><td>conspicuous consumption<br>炫耀性消费</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bright red jacket made him conspicuous in the gray winter crowd.  他鲜红的夹克让他在灰暗的冬日人群中格外显眼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 remarkable</td><td>/rɪˈmɑːrkəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，引人注目的</td><td>a remarkable achievement<br>非凡的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Finishing the bridge in a year was a remarkable achievement.  一年建成这座桥是非凡的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 striking</td><td>/ˈstraɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人注目的，显著的</td><td>a striking resemblance<br>惊人的相似</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a striking contrast between the two sisters' styles.  两姐妹的风格形成鲜明的对比。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>nonentity</b></td><td>/nɑːˈnentəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不重要的人</td><td>a political nonentity<br>政坛上的无名之辈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Once a political nonentity, he suddenly became the party's most quoted voice.  他曾是政坛的无名之辈，突然成了党内被引用最多的声音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cipher</td><td>/ˈsaɪfər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>密码</td><td>break the cipher<br>破解密码</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Codebreakers took months to break the enemy cipher.  破译人员花了几个月才破解敌方的密码。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 half-pint</td><td>/ˈhæf ˈpaɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>小个子，微不足道的人</td><td>a cheeky half-pint<br>厚脸皮的小个子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That half-pint of a clerk dared to argue with the boss.  那个不起眼的小职员竟敢跟老板争辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 insignificancy</del></td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>微不足道的人或事</td><td>a mere insignificancy<br>不过是微末小事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the complaint as a mere insignificancy.  他把这投诉当作微末小事而不理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lightweight</td><td>/ˈlaɪtweɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>无足轻重的人；轻量的</td><td>a political lightweight<br>政治上的小角色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics dismissed him as a political lightweight.  批评者把他当作政治上的小角色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nullity</td><td>/ˈnʌlɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无效；不存在</td><td>a legal nullity<br>法律上的无效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court declared the contract a complete nullity.  法院宣布该合同完全无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 snippersnapper</del></td><td>/ˈsnɪpərsnæpər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自命不凡的小人物</td><td>an impudent snippersnapper<br>无礼的狂妄小子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sergeant called the recruit an impudent snippersnapper.  老班长骂那新兵是无礼的狂妄小子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 twerp</td><td>/twɜrp/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>讨厌的小人物，蠢货</td><td>a little twerp<br>讨厌的小家伙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stop behaving like a twerp and apologize to her.  别再像个蠢货那样，去向她道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whippersnapper</td><td>/ˈwɪpərsnæpər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自以为是的年轻人</td><td>a young whippersnapper<br>自以为是的小年轻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some young whippersnapper tried to lecture the veteran engineer.  某个自以为是的小年轻竟教训起老工程师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 zero</td><td>/ˈzɪroʊ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无价值的人；零</td><td>a complete zero<br>彻底的无名之辈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In that crowd he felt like a complete zero.  在那群人中他觉得自己毫无分量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 zilch</td><td>/ˈzɪltʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>毫无，零；无名小卒</td><td>know zilch about it<br>对此一无所知</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I know zilch about repairing car engines.  我对修汽车发动机一无所知。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mogul</td><td>/ˈmoʊɡəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>巨头，大人物</td><td>a media mogul<br>传媒巨头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A media mogul bought the struggling newspaper last spring.  一位传媒巨头去年春天买下了这家困境中的报纸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 big shot</td><td>/ˈbɪɡ ˌʃɑt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>大人物，要人</td><td>a big shot in banking<br>银行界的大人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some big shot from head office visited the branch today.  总部来的某位大人物今天视察了分行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 big wheel</td><td>/ˈbɪɡ ˈwil/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>有权势的人物</td><td>a big wheel in the party<br>党内的实权人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father was a big wheel in the local union.  她父亲是当地工会的实权人物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bigwig</td><td>/ˈbɪɡˌwɪɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>大人物，权贵</td><td>a local bigwig<br>当地的大人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every local bigwig turned up for the ribbon cutting.  当地每位大人物都来参加剪彩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eminence</td><td>/ˈemənəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卓越，杰出；显赫人物</td><td>rise to eminence<br>崭露头角，声名显赫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He achieved eminence as a surgeon before he turned forty.  他四十岁前就成了声名卓著的外科医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 figure</td><td>/ˈfɪɡjər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>身材，身影；数字；人物</td><td>a slender figure<br>苗条的身材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tall figure appeared suddenly at the end of the corridor.  一个高大的身影突然出现在走廊尽头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 magnate</td><td>/ˈmæɡneɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>巨头，大亨</td><td>a shipping magnate<br>航运大亨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A shipping magnate funded the whole research station.  一位航运大亨资助了整个研究站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 personage</td><td>/ˈpɜrsənɪdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>要人，显要人物</td><td>a distinguished personage<br>显要人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A distinguished personage opened the exhibition without a speech.  一位显要人物没有讲话就为展览开幕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 somebody</td><td>/ˈsʌmbɑːdi/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>重要人物，有名气的人</td><td>become somebody<br>成为有头有脸的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He left the village determined to become somebody.  他离开村子，决心成为有头有脸的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>nonplus</b></del></td><td>/ˌnɑːnˈplʌs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使迷惑，使困窘</td><td>be completely nonplussed<br>完全不知所措</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her strange reply left the whole panel nonplussed.  她奇怪的回答让整个评审团都困惑不解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confound</td><td>/kənˈfaʊnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；证明为假，证伪</td><td>confound the critics<br>让批评者困惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The late victory confounded the critics who had predicted failure.  最后的胜利让预言失败的批评者困惑不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 confuse</td><td>/kənˈfjuːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使困惑；混淆</td><td>confuse the issue<br>混淆问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vague instructions confused nearly everyone in the training class.  含糊的说明让培训班几乎所有人都困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 discomfit</del></td><td>/dɪsˈkʌmfɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬；阻碍</td><td>discomfit an opponent<br>使对手窘迫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blunt question visibly discomfited the confident speaker.  这个直率的问题明显让自信的演讲者局促不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disconcert</td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈsɜːrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不安，使慌乱</td><td>disconcert the audience<br>使观众不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His long silences disconcerted everyone at the table.  他长时间的沉默让餐桌上所有人都不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 discountenance</del></td><td>/dɪsˈkaʊntənəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不赞成；使窘迫</td><td>discountenance such behavior<br>不赞成这种行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee discountenanced any further spending on the project.  委员会不赞成该项目再有任何支出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mortify</td><td>/ˈmɔːrtɪˌfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使羞愧，使难堪</td><td>be mortified with embarrassment<br>羞愧得无地自容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was mortified to find her mistake printed in the report.  发现自己的错误印在报告里，她羞愧难当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abash</del></td><td>/əˈbæʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬，使羞愧</td><td>be abashed by praise<br>因受赞扬而羞怯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked abashed when everyone applauded his clumsy speech.  当大家为他笨拙的演讲鼓掌时，他显得很窘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faze</td><td>/feɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使慌乱，使窘迫</td><td>nothing seems to faze him<br>似乎没什么能扰乱他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The huge crowd did not faze the young pianist at all.  庞大的观众丝毫没让这位年轻钢琴家慌乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluster</td><td>/ˈflʌstər/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使慌乱，使紧张</td><td>get flustered easily<br>容易慌乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unexpected questions always fluster him during interviews.  面试中意外的问题总让他慌乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>numinous</b></td><td>/ˈnuːmɪnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的；神圣的</td><td>a numinous presence<br>神秘的存在感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The empty stone chapel had a numinous quality that silenced every visitor.  空荡的石砌小教堂有种神秘气息，让每位来访者都沉默下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magic</td><td>/ˈmædʒɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>魔力的；魔法</td><td>a magic spell<br>魔咒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children believed a magic spell protected the old oak.  孩子们相信有魔咒护着那棵老橡树。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magical</td><td>/ˈmædʒɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>魔法的，神奇的</td><td>a magical atmosphere<br>神奇的氛围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Candlelight gave the small chapel a magical atmosphere.  烛光让这座小教堂有了神奇的氛围。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mystic</td><td>/ˈmɪstɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>神秘的，玄妙的</td><td>a mystic experience<br>神秘的体验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old poem describes a mystic union with the natural world.  这首古诗描写了与自然界的神秘合一。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 occult</td><td>/əˈkʌlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>occult knowledge<br>玄奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript is filled with occult knowledge that scholars still cannot decipher.  这份手稿满是学者至今无法解读的玄奥知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weird</td><td>/ˈwɪrd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>怪异的，离奇的</td><td>a weird coincidence<br>离奇的巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film has a weird ending that nobody in the theater understood.  这部电影的结尾很怪，影院里没人看懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>notch</b></td><td>/nɑːtʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（山间）隘口，缺口</td><td>a notch in the ridge<br>山脊上的隘口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trail climbed toward a high notch between the two peaks.  小径向着两座山峰之间的高处隘口攀升。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attain</td><td>/əˈteɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>达到，获得</td><td>attain a goal<br>达成目标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She attained her doctorate before turning twenty-six.  她在二十六岁前取得了博士学位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bag</td><td>/ˈbæɡ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得，猎获</td><td>bag a victory<br>赢得一场胜利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rookie bagged three medals at his first meet.  这名新手在首场比赛就拿下三枚奖牌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chalk up</td><td>/ˈtʃɔk ˈʌp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>取得，记下（成绩）</td><td>chalk up a win<br>取得一场胜利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team chalked up its fifth win of the season.  该队取得本季第五场胜利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clock up</td><td>/ˈklɑk ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>累计达到（数量）</td><td>clock up the miles<br>累计里程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He clocked up thirty thousand miles in one year.  他一年累计跑了三万英里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gain</td><td>/ˈɡeɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得，赢得</td><td>gain recognition<br>获得认可</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essays gained recognition well beyond the campus.  她的文章赢得了远超校园的认可。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hit</td><td>/ˈhɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>成功而流行的事物；打击</td><td>a smash hit<br>轰动一时的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first single became an unexpected hit across three countries.  她的首支单曲在三个国家意外大热。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 log</td><td>/ˈlɔːɡ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>记录，登记</td><td>log the results<br>记录结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nurses log every dose in the patient's chart.  护士把每次用药都记录在病历上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 make</td><td>/ˈmeɪk/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>制造，做成；使成为</td><td>make a wooden chair<br>做一把木椅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My grandfather could make a wooden chair with only simple tools.  我祖父只用简单工具就能做出一把木椅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rack up</td><td>/ˈræk ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>累积，取得（大量）</td><td>rack up debts<br>累积债务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The store racked up huge losses over the winter.  这家店整个冬天累积了巨额亏损。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ring up</td><td>/ˈrɪŋ ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>记下（销售额），取得</td><td>ring up sales<br>记下销售额</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The shop rang up record sales during the festival.  这家店在节日期间创下销售记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 score</td><td>/ˈskɔːr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>得分，取得</td><td>score a point<br>得一分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She scored the winning point in the final second.  她在最后一秒拿下制胜分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 win</td><td>/ˈwɪn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>赢得，获胜</td><td>win an award<br>获奖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their small documentary won an award in Berlin.  他们的小纪录片在柏林获奖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>novel</b></td><td>/ˈnɑːvl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新奇的</td><td>a novel approach<br>新奇的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her novel approach to the old problem surprised even the senior researchers.  她对这个老问题的新奇方法连资深研究员都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unaccustomed</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈkʌstəmd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不习惯的；不常见的</td><td>unaccustomed to the cold<br>不习惯寒冷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unaccustomed to the cold, he wore three sweaters indoors.  他不习惯寒冷，在室内穿了三件毛衣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfamiliar</td><td>/ˌʌnfəˈmɪljər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不熟悉的，陌生的</td><td>an unfamiliar face<br>一张陌生的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The street names here were all unfamiliar to me.  这里的街名对我来说都很陌生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unheard-of</td><td>/ʌnˈhɜrd ʌv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>前所未闻的，闻所未闻的</td><td>an unheard-of price<br>闻所未闻的价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting sold for an unheard-of price that night.  那晚这幅画以闻所未闻的价格成交。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unknown</td><td>/ˌʌnˈnoʊn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未知的，不为人知的</td><td>an unknown author<br>无名的作者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The diary was written by an unknown author in 1830.  这本日记由一位无名作者于1830年写成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unprecedented</td><td>/ʌnˈpresɪdentɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>前所未有的</td><td>unprecedented growth<br>前所未有的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city saw unprecedented growth in a single decade.  这座城市在十年间实现前所未有的增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timeworn</td><td>/ˈtaɪmˌwɔːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧的，老套的</td><td>a timeworn saying<br>老掉牙的谚语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He comforted us with timeworn advice about patience and hard work.  他用关于耐心与勤奋的老套建议安慰我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 familiar</td><td>/fəˈmɪljər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟悉的，通晓的</td><td>familiar with the subject<br>熟悉这门学科</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her voice sounded strangely familiar over the phone.  她的声音在电话里听起来奇怪地熟悉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hackneyed</td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 time-honored</td><td>/ˈtaɪm ˌɑnərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>历史悠久的，因古老而受尊重的</td><td>a time-honored custom<br>历史悠久的习俗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They opened the harvest with a time-honored village custom.  他们以历史悠久的村俗开始收获季。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tired</td><td>/ˈtaɪərd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>疲倦的；厌倦的</td><td>tired of waiting<br>厌倦了等待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By midnight everyone was too tired to argue further.  到午夜时，所有人都累得无法再争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 warmed-over</td><td>/ˌwɔrmd ˈoʊvər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，老调重弹的</td><td>warmed-over arguments<br>老调重弹的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech offered only warmed-over arguments from last year.  这篇讲话只是去年论点的老调重弹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>noxious</b></td><td>/ˈnɑːkʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，有毒的；产生强烈厌恶的</td><td>noxious fumes<br>有毒的烟气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Noxious fumes from the factory forced the school to close for two days.  工厂排出的有毒烟气迫使学校停课两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unwholesome</td><td>/ʌnˈhoʊlsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，不健康的</td><td>an unwholesome influence<br>不良的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her parents thought the crowd an unwholesome influence.  她父母认为那群人是不良影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baneful</td><td>/ˈbeɪnfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，有毒的</td><td>a baneful habit<br>一个有害的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Gossip had a baneful effect on the whole department.  流言对整个部门产生了有害的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deleterious</td><td>/ˌdeləˈtɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的</td><td>deleterious effects on health<br>对健康的有害影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long night shifts had a deleterious effect on the nurses' health.  长期夜班对护士们的健康产生了有害影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 detrimental</td><td>/ˌdetrəˈmentəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，不利的</td><td>detrimental to your health<br>对你的健康有害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too much screen time is detrimental to a child's eyesight.  过多看屏幕对孩子的视力有害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nocuous</del></td><td>/ˈnɑːkjuəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的，有毒的</td><td>nocuous fumes<br>有害的烟气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Factory chimneys released nocuous gases into the evening air.  工厂烟囱把有害气体排入夜晚的空气中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pernicious</td><td>/pərˈnɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有害的</td><td>a pernicious rumor<br>极有害的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A pernicious rumor about the school spread quickly through the town.  一则关于学校的极有害谣言迅速在镇上传开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wicked</td><td>/ˈwɪkɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的</td><td>a wicked scheme<br>邪恶的计谋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A wicked scheme to seize the estate was uncovered by the lawyer.  律师揭穿了一个夺取遗产的邪恶计谋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abhorrent</td><td>/æbˈhɔːrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>abhorrent to most people<br>令多数人厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The practice is abhorrent to anyone with basic decency.  这种做法令任何有基本良知的人厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abominable</td><td>/əˈbɑːmənəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，极糟的</td><td>abominable behavior<br>可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His abominable behavior at dinner shocked the guests.  他晚餐时可憎的举止让客人震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appalling</td><td>/əˈpɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>骇人的，糟糕透顶的</td><td>appalling living conditions<br>骇人的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers lived in appalling conditions close to the mine.  工人们住在矿区附近骇人的条件中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awful</td><td>/ˈɑːfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>糟糕的，可怕的</td><td>an awful smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An awful smell came from the forgotten lunchbox.  被遗忘的饭盒里飘出难闻的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disgusting</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a disgusting smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was left in a disgusting state after the party.  聚会后厨房脏得让人恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distasteful</td><td>/dɪˈsteɪstfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人不快的，讨厌的</td><td>a distasteful joke<br>令人不快的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found the whole discussion deeply distasteful.  她觉得整场讨论令人极不舒服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreadful</td><td>/ˈdredfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，糟透的</td><td>a dreadful mistake<br>可怕的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a dreadful headache and went home early.  她头疼得厉害，很早就回家了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foul</td><td>/ˈfaʊl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>玷污（名誉）；弄脏</td><td>foul one's good name<br>玷污自己的好名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scandal fouled the good name he had spent decades building.  这桩丑闻玷污了他数十年积累的好名声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fulsome</td><td>/ˈfʊlsəm/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分的，令人反感的</td><td>fulsome praise<br>过分的赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fulsome praise embarrassed everyone in the room.  他过分的赞美让屋里每个人尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gross</td><td>/ˈɡroʊs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于肥胖的；粗俗的</td><td>a gross, bloated figure<br>臃肿肥胖的身形</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The caricature gave the mayor a gross, swollen belly.  那幅漫画给市长画了个臃肿肥大的肚子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrid</td><td>/ˈhɔːrəd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，讨厌的</td><td>a horrid experience<br>糟糕的经历</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The flight home turned into a horrid experience.  回家的航班变成一段糟糕的经历。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loathsome</td><td>/ˈloʊðsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>a loathsome smell<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A loathsome smell rose from the neglected drain.  无人清理的下水道升起令人厌恶的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nasty</td><td>/ˈnæsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，肮脏的；恶劣的</td><td>a nasty smell<br>难闻的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A nasty stain spread slowly across the pale carpet.  一块难看的污渍在浅色地毯上慢慢蔓延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nauseating</td><td>/ˈnɔːziˌeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人恶心的</td><td>a nauseating stench<br>令人恶心的臭味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nauseating stench drove everyone out of the kitchen.  令人恶心的臭味把所有人赶出厨房。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noisome</td><td>/ˈnɔɪsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的；令人极厌恶的</td><td>a noisome odor<br>一股恶臭气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers refused to enter the noisome tunnel without masks.  工人们不戴口罩不肯进入那条恶臭的隧道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obnoxious</td><td>/əbˈnɑːkʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极讨厌的，令人反感的</td><td>an obnoxious smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His obnoxious jokes ruined the whole dinner party.  他讨人厌的玩笑毁了整场晚宴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 odious</td><td>/ˈoʊdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可憎的，令人厌恶的</td><td>an odious task<br>讨厌的差事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Comparing the two writers seems both odious and pointless.  比较这两位作家显得可憎又无意义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repellent</td><td>/rɪˈpelənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>令人厌恶的；驱虫剂</td><td>a repellent odor<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found his arrogant remarks utterly repellent and left early.  我觉得他傲慢的言论令人极其厌恶，于是提前离开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repugnant</td><td>/rɪˈpʌɡnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>morally repugnant behavior<br>道德上可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most readers found the cruelty of that character morally repugnant and unforgivable.  多数读者觉得那个角色的残忍在道德上令人厌恶，不可宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repulsive</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a repulsive sight<br>令人恶心的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rotting fish on the beach was absolutely repulsive.  沙滩上腐烂的鱼令人极其恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revolting</td><td>/rɪˈvoʊltɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a revolting smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A revolting smell came from the abandoned refrigerator in the yard.  院里那台废弃冰箱散出一股恶心的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scandalous</td><td>/ˈskændələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>引起反感的，丢脸的</td><td>a scandalous waste of money<br>令人反感的浪费</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the project a scandalous waste of public money.  批评者称这个项目是对公共资金令人反感的浪费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 beneficial</td><td>/ˌbenəˈfɪʃəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益的，有利的</td><td>beneficial to your health<br>对健康有益</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular walking is beneficial to both your heart and mood.  经常步行对心脏和情绪都有益。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 salubrious</td><td>/səˈluːbriəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的</td><td>a salubrious mountain climate<br>有益健康的山地气候</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors sent him to a salubrious mountain village to recover his strength.  医生把他送到有益健康的山村去恢复体力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 anodyne</td><td>/ˈænədaɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>anodyne remarks<br>不痛不痒的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The spokesman gave anodyne answers that offended nobody at all.  发言人给出的回答不痛不痒，谁也没得罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 benign</td><td>/bɪˈnaɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a benign influence<br>有益无害的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in policy proved benign for most small farmers.  事实证明，政策变化对大多数小农户是无害的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmless</td><td>/ˈhɑːrmləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，不会造成伤害的</td><td>a harmless little joke<br>无伤大雅的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The snake looks frightening but is completely harmless to humans.  这条蛇看起来可怕，但对人完全无害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 hurtless</del></td><td>/ˈhɜrtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的</td><td>a hurtless remedy<br>无害的疗法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The herb is hurtless even in fairly large doses.  这种草药即使较大剂量也无害。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 innocent</td><td>/ˈɪnəsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，无恶意的；无辜的</td><td>an innocent remark<br>无恶意的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What began as an innocent joke ended in a serious quarrel.  一句本无恶意的玩笑最后引发了激烈争吵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 innocuous</td><td>/ɪˈnɑːkjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的；不会引起敌意的</td><td>an innocuous question<br>一个无恶意的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 What seemed an innocuous question turned out to reveal her deepest secret.  看似无恶意的问题最终却揭露了她最深的秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inoffensive</td><td>/ˌɪnəˈfensɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无害的，不讨人厌的</td><td>inoffensive background music<br>不讨人厌的背景音乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is bland but inoffensive enough for family viewing.  这部电影平淡，但足够温和，适合全家观看。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 safe</td><td>/ˈseɪf/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>安全的，可靠的</td><td>a safe choice<br>稳妥的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Taking the train is the safe choice in this weather.  这种天气坐火车是稳妥的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
